- **[Wainwright Manager](https://x.ai/bot/TqyhVfSrAYZ-xQSrD_x1A)** by @itsryanlenk — Works as the hiring desk for a fleet of assistants: you pick the role you need from a single list, and it reads the specification over for problems before anything is created. It then stands the agent up, checks the profile is correct, and passes across the wake routine, so the new hire arrives ready to take work.
- **[Remote Grok Build](https://x.ai/bot/dLYpR1sSkG0VLa8-faEgX)** by @FrankYin17 — Reaches the builder installed on your home machine from the app, so work started on a phone picks up on the computer holding the code. Projects can be split across separate workers, with a side channel for running several turns alongside one another, and none of it needs a tunnel or a public address.
- **[Ops Knowledge Observer](https://x.ai/bot/CIgxhKdiPj7KflehWkaEr)** by @StephenRuhe — Shared memory for several operations bots rather than a worker in its own right. A setup interview records who owns which area and where the EDI and TMS data lives, and from then on the team draws context from one place instead of every bot hoarding private notes. It reads and answers; it changes nothing.
- **[Team Coordinador](https://x.ai/bot/rWI7zYaHEPW3Ek6ODy8sk)** by @LordCocoro — A Spanish-language coordinator for a Grok team of specialists. Work is assigned so nobody strays into a teammate's area, one vault is kept for the whole team to read from, and the week closes with a Sunday digest. No personal data is stored, and the vault plus webhook inbox are set at import.
- **[Grottle](https://x.ai/bot/YvFrCr_VlFW_8PxaoFv_L)** by @BCornTexas — Tell it your usage percentage and where the weekly window resets, and it hands back a read-out that is easier to judge at a glance than the raw number. Where the reset moment can be pinned down, the exact date and time come with it. The bot only measures; it never changes your account, your plan or your spend.
- **[POUNCE](https://x.ai/bot/7jLpLuCPbki8Q_nRrPN18)** by @teeRex247 — Once a day it sweeps what AI companies are offering by way of contests, grant programs, free credits and hackathons. Every deadline is re-checked on the page that announced it, and anything you have already seen is held back. What reaches you is a short list of genuinely new chances to apply.
- **[Ship Desk](https://x.ai/bot/thMhD-rbJ0Osd274RGohH)** by @usgworld — Walks a product from staging to production one locked promote at a time, so a release only goes live when you say so. Checkout stays fail-closed until you explicitly turn it on, which keeps accidental buy buttons off a launch while it is still moving.
- **[Devil's Advocate](https://x.ai/bot/_Gxt_kJLC4XjNPxzqV89h)** by @codofliess — Risk advisor that cuts before money, live tokens, or deploy. Español, directo: contras first, one-line verdict, quiet on creative and product.
- **[Astra Afterburner](https://x.ai/bot/EEQXiBDbM6YJG1eedy8d4)** by @SacredFolio — Scans a repository for questions worth investigating and holds them in a queue. When your coding assistant hits its usage ceiling for the day, that queue is handed to a browser session on your other paid plan instead of going nowhere, so a subscription you already fund does not sit idle.
- **[Mobile App Autopilot](https://x.ai/bot/a3EwtQPjFkXZaPZEMGHgY)** by @imsorryindustry — It turns your idea into a written spec, then hands the build to cloud agents who work as pull requests you review before anything merges. It runs the builds and assembles the store materials for App Store and Google Play. You stay in the loop at review time while setup and paperwork are handled around you.
- **[agent-notify](https://x.ai/bot/alHx8zJA0xXEN8FT1HDAc)** by @SudoLabsNZ — A notification sidekick for people running agents and automations: it sends an email only when an event genuinely needs a human, instead of streaming every status line at you. The mail flow is one-way out, so nothing in your mailbox is ever read. Setup is a one-click affair on Cloudflare, costing minutes.
- **[Z1 CAM Shop](https://x.ai/bot/4TxZ2KVW5aM6bfOleofNb)** by @msmage — Describe the job and it works the cut like a shop assistant: which tool, what surface speed, how deep, how the stock should be held, and where the fixturing will fight you. It finishes with a setup sheet, so a question ends as a page you can take to the machine. For desktop mill owners cutting real parts.
- **[CASE](https://x.ai/bot/QnnJBu_7kZzuaR3AP1mFv)** by @ibuildthings — Show it the artifact — the code, the design, the plan — and it reviews the real thing rather than a summary. The posture is quiet and precise: flag the weak points in the implementation and the architecture, name the risk, and let you decide. Built to run alongside a build, not to take it over.
- **[Live Pages](https://x.ai/bot/ignLvLJlOxOIenZPr-tTA)** by @darkart — Ask for a small tool and it builds an actual page — a table, a form, a checklist — on a machine of its own, reachable from a phone or laptop on your Tailscale network, not the open internet. Tap a button or submit the form and the page changes, your tab reloading to show it. A stopgap until bots can draw their own UI.
- **[Right Model, Effort & Prompt](https://x.ai/bot/T3hutFteALP3ObLPxQJpn)** by @Foundation_Bots — Describe a task and it names a model and effort setting to suit it across OpenAI, Anthropic, Grok, Meta, and Google, so premium capacity stops going to work a cheaper model handles. It reworks your prompt too, before you ever hit send, and weekday mornings bring a note when fresh model news changes its pick.
- **[Precision 3D Helper](https://x.ai/bot/bna2wMqlfX6-F4TBmAEBJ)** by @srinimaram — It covers the front desk of a small printing shop: answering the customer questions that repeat weekly, quoting jobs with quantity breaks worked out, and steering each print to a sensible material and colour. It also preps models for the printer and helps troubleshoot Bambu Lab machines when something goes wrong.
- **[Token Finder](https://x.ai/bot/1olDKisJ4GIsykzyxADtD)** by @CaseyChesh — Once a week it goes through the routines behind your bots and looks for quota that is sitting idle. You get a plain list of where the savings would come from before anything is touched, and nothing changes until you approve it. Freed-up room means you can run more bots on the same plan.
- **[iOS Bot](https://x.ai/bot/RIPO6abjI60-ZXRFAj9hG)** by @JaimeBubblehead — Paired with your Mac, it shepherds a Swift or SwiftUI project from rough sketches to a TestFlight build, then handles the iPhone-side setup for what it made. Submission, release, and spending are all blocked until you personally say so.
- **[Apple Desk](https://x.ai/bot/vVST27b0qMvGLrEfxZ3nj)** by @Phil_Holland — It lives on your Mac and reaches the Apple apps through their command-line tools, covering reminders, calendar events, notes, contacts, and messages. Setup is guided and each permission gets its own click of approval before anything is granted.
- **[How To Build Any App From Idea To Shelf](https://x.ai/bot/y_5NbvloRD4oA-Fi3S7Nv)** by @TripVoxel — It works as the product manager on your idea, producing the blueprint, the money model, the loops that keep users around, the referral mechanics, and the handoff docs a team needs. Rewards schemes wait on purpose until the product can actually carry them.
- **[Authenticator App](https://x.ai/bot/etB2IDpbLPT-ZW4IIewQK)** by @refreshinga — Store your two-factor secrets in one place and pull the current six-digit code whenever a sign-in asks. A different assistant can receive a code only for services on your allow list, and the secret itself stays masked through every exchange.
- **[音声操作PCアシスタント（スマホで挙動確認可能）](https://x.ai/bot/hjYT5yVxhY5d548aSa1UL)** by @XARX_ZERO — Tell your computer what to do from another room and it listens. Routine commands like muting run as a local script so almost nothing reaches the AI, while bigger asks go to the bot and the result lands on your phone. Risky operations are blocked, the access key is stored encrypted on the PC, setup is one manual step.
- **[Bloks](https://x.ai/bot/w4Rvgudmoi8AEb0FZ3PGg)** by @NadineCodes — Takes any brief - hackathon entry, client ask, internal push - and carries it stage by stage to something a builder can pick up: frame the problem, research it, scope it, generate ideas, write the spec, review it, then hand off. Documents stay in Notion as it goes, so the team always sees the current version.
- **[iCloud](https://x.ai/bot/cXeisxhmz3YpvnWVH1k0F)** by @CyberZack42 — A community-built connector that wires Apple's iCloud services into the app: calendar, mail, contacts and reminders. Installation checkboxes let you pick which of the four you want, and a single Apple app-specific password handles the sign-in for whichever you choose.
- **[Pip (QA)](https://x.ai/bot/o2tkDy7VSw45sbWViOO16)** by @EmergentContent — Runs an app or game through two passes and reports what each one found. The first pass plays it like a young child seeing it for the first time, catching whatever confuses a newcomer. The second goes through it with an adult tester's discipline. You get reproducible bug reports sorted by severity.
- **[Cloudflare GitHub Site Bot](https://x.ai/bot/wuX9f9WZMmY8e1EQ_eB8A)** by @xPrivateUser — A walkthrough for getting a website live on GitHub and Cloudflare Workers, whether you are starting fresh or migrating something that already exists. It covers the whole path from accounts that do not exist yet, past SSH keys and API tokens, on to the deploy itself — the fiddly credential steps stop sinking launches.
- **[Deploy Desk](https://x.ai/bot/7n0xNJQkJya50-WawGibg)** by @DJDEEZ — A launch desk for tiny software products: it watches the deployed site, queues social posts with generated visuals so the product's account never goes quiet, and drafts outreach for you to send. Every deploy gets journaled next to its rollback path, and nothing ships without your sign-off.
- **[Greenlight](https://x.ai/bot/CjabaVv2icvagIumZlevu)** by @MrSaneApps — For indie developers, review day is where releases quietly die. This one reads your app or extension against the review rules of the Apple Mac store, Setapp and the Chrome Web Store, flags whatever looks like a rejection waiting to happen, and hands you a fix list to clear before you press upload.
- **[Requirement Engineer Bot](https://x.ai/bot/5KADFS8AIIDOlow5tS34Z)** by @FishxCD — Hand it a brief and it returns a proper requirements specification: top-level needs broken down into implementation-level detail, each item traceable to the objective it serves. The structure lets a downstream bot or developer build from it and verify delivery against what was asked. It writes specs, not code.
- **[Construction Manager Bot](https://x.ai/bot/QrJpjStFV2WBIaI8wirO6)** by @HenryBeagle808 — Built for a phone on an active civil site: photograph a problem and it produces a structured card describing the issue, with a draft RFI to match. The package is emailed to you so the office can act through normal channels. Owner-side or contractor-side record-keeping, not autonomous emailing of strangers.
- **[Ryan App Builder](https://x.ai/bot/acSvd_2n8QnlnAjJebkvy)** by @BlakeKing777 — Describe what you want in plain English and it designs and builds a small working app that pins to your phone's home screen, no code and no app store required. Aimed at people who have never built anything; the sharer's examples were a chore chart and a team schedule. Each step is confirmed before moving on.
- **[Pit Crew](https://x.ai/bot/Sm0GZs7Hhf7kNV7tHCUN1)** by @scottxmetcalf — When the machine your bots share starts running hot on memory or storage, this one steps in and takes the pressure off before work grinds to a halt. It also guards the slim update route - keeping git and plugin fetches healthy - so marketplace and skill updates keep arriving on time.
- **[Bug Reproduction](https://x.ai/bot/s2d62197e15bd11a1bd63)** by @DenisLabelle — Converts messy bug threads into repro packs an engineer can trust: it walks the same path the reporter took, in staging, captures the moment the failure actually fires, and packages the steps with the evidence attached. Debugging starts from a confirmed reproduction instead of a confusing screenshot and a shrug.
- **[Longhand](https://x.ai/bot/N53am-rfjXPCA2UJ91kEG)** by @VMVLAX — Runs on one principle: plain files are the source of truth and the bot's own memory is only a cache, so rules and decisions stay readable and diffable. Routines for inbox watch, sweeps, a nightly memory commit and a Sunday audit arrive switched off — you enable the ones you want.
- **[Notion](https://x.ai/bot/-p3E1-AtfuoYitjQaBMVC)** by @scottxmetcalf — Treats a team's Notion workspace as its source of truth: ask a question and it answers from the pages that exist, pointing at the page behind each claim. Where it spots a gap it drafts the missing update for you to place. No write happens on its own; publishing, moving, archiving or removing a page all need your OK.
- **[Jev](https://x.ai/bot/JQ4PSTdVDZdIOa8qwfP0G)** by @NeuralCatAccel — A triage seat for teams that already run TypeSafe Jev. Incoming items are sorted into a category, given a severity, and answered with a plain go or no-go, all through typed calls instead of free text. The prerequisite is real: the Jev side has to be wired into LiteLLM first, and any other call path means extra setup.
- **[Piper | ETL & Pipelines](https://x.ai/bot/eQ3bzQTxNshSUzytoh29P)** by @damianoredem — The plumbing seat of a data fleet. With the schema agreed, its job is to create or move the event-log store and then keep it supplied — every source that connects gets its output normalised and loaded into a destination the user owns, not one the tool keeps for itself.
- **[Top Coder](https://x.ai/bot/dwuB7MmlnrkcXYtyOp-Hk)** by @EricBuess — Codes against a fixed list of acceptance criteria, and for each one it supplies a test that demonstrates the behaviour instead of an assertion that claims it. Before anything is called finished, a separate checker goes back over the result independently. Done, here, means verified rather than merely written.
- **[Summarize Bot](https://x.ai/bot/9vk3KOm7wJzYVXlyKXxj7)** by @_VincentPeng — Point at any URL, YouTube, podcast, or file — get a short gist in chat via the summarize CLI. On-demand; never invents without the CLI.
- **[Hopper](https://x.ai/bot/_zMsp35NS2zl-Kp1uIegB)** by @deaguiarza — Funnel.io specialist: sources, custom dims/metrics via the fields API, explorers, dashboard XHR maps, and BigQuery raw exports for client onboard.
- **[obsidian](https://x.ai/bot/tukhb8f4XyUJFkRWlHWaz)** by @weeb3dev — CLI-first Obsidian specialist for your Grok Bot computer. Owns a local vault as a second brain — create, edit, link, and search Markdown with Obsidian.
- **[Bouncer (Fleet)](https://x.ai/bot/fcAnJJxw7NU_zL0Zq6Ixo)** by @pcoxproductions — Point it at the bots, skills and routines already running in your workspace and it reviews what each one does with your data. It hunts for outbound calls nobody mentioned, quietly collected credentials, and telemetry that serves the builder more than you. Findings come back labelled pass, conditional or block.
- **[Packet Wrangler Range Rider](https://x.ai/bot/QeblYPmzNFIbeCJR_dKyS)** by @SLamouroux — Keeps an eye on a hosted setup and reports on four things: uptime, certificate health, how the pages score on web-vitals checks, and whether any content has gone stale. Fixes are written up as proposals rather than applied, and production is never touched without your agreement. Checks run on a schedule.
- **[Call](https://x.ai/bot/aItvmBN1hl5uR6kHvSXrG)** by @iserflott — Aimed at developers wiring real telephony into an agent. A SIP bridge hands the audio to a live voice model, which follows the script, reacts to events mid-conversation and ends the call when the goal is met or the exchange is going nowhere. Add an approval step before it dials anything.
- **[Little x](https://x.ai/bot/KTMYJjzan8cuclz2lv1Rz)** by @DonaldMoor91672 — Functions as an ops desk for the stretch of the day when you are nowhere near the app. Send a command by email and it drafts X replies, holds them for your approval before anything is posted, retrieves the files you request and pushes alerts to your phone. The market alerts are read-only, with no trading involved.
- **[Estack](https://x.ai/bot/R0acF6Pmp8YewSZm6fA-D)** by @robannand — Will not let a coding task begin loosely: the objective, the boundaries and the finish line get fixed before anything runs. Repository work then goes to Cursor Cloud Agents on your account, which waits on the returned artifacts. Review is evidence-based, judged on the tests and pull request, not the summary.
- **[Steve](https://x.ai/bot/tVFpcwYEBXW4ReAFni6Cn)** by @IAmMichaelSweet — Runs the engineering cadence for a multi-brand bakery operation in the Scrum master seat. Part of the work is reviewing which connectors people genuinely use, and part is making the build-versus-buy call on new tooling. It pushes back on anything that looks entertaining but returns nothing measurable to the operation.
- **[OwnPhoneBot](https://x.ai/bot/yaQTHVqOMscKBYYDAfNoP)** by @lafraia — Connects a real telephone number to an assistant by taking the fiddly provisioning off your hands: it signs you up with the telephone provider and installs an Asterisk server so the two ends can talk. Ownership stays with you, and so do the bills, since the provider and the model vendor both charge you directly.
- **[RobIT](https://x.ai/bot/0BbPklegaSLcUqp0CVXda)** by @CrystalJ613 — Treats a sluggish Windows machine as a job to fix, not a chat about fixing. It hunts down what sits behind freezes and crashes, strips out the half-removed antivirus or browser add-ons ordinary uninstalls leave behind, and works through why a shop or government portal refuses to load, driving its own Chrome profile.
- **[Exec Daily Ops](https://x.ai/bot/vIIc2EV8FFZ3g6W8JUgoZ)** by @kumarkandgule — Condenses the overnight mail and ticket queue into a three-line summary and a capped list for today, then tracks sprint health and AI spending against the budget you set. Draft replies come back in your voice and stay unsent until you send them, and the writeups are tidy enough for a Monday meeting.
- **[Taller](https://x.ai/bot/cYZG2v8vG9Q7Fsds3Ickp)** by @FrancoE114696 — Dev-tools inbox for Grok Bot. Drop a repo, npm, CLI, or link and it saves a short Spanish card under taller/; ask later and it recommends only from that.
- **[Doorcheck](https://x.ai/bot/bDhYfDe0sPlaVI9zMmZ4e)** by @the_Arow_H — Hand it a template you are about to add and it reads the recipe — instructions, requested skills, plugin asks — as material to weigh, not orders to obey. The verdict comes back as block, allow with limits, or clear. Green means no significant install-time findings, which is narrower than calling it safe.
- **[White-Hat Security Tester](https://x.ai/bot/TSFkOGld9o51h_qXGOioI)** by @agentic_joe — Scoped strictly to applications you own or are authorized to test. It works a structured checklist of the usual break-in routes — authentication weaknesses, access-control gaps, injection points, business-logic flaws — and reports what it finds so the holes get closed first. Defensive testing, not a scanning toy.
- **[Stack Cartographer](https://x.ai/bot/NjIGGseAWM32s65KGoqOK)** by @psiesa — Point it at your working machine and it produces a survey of what is there: the applications, the services, the tooling and the connections between them. While mapping, it also watches for credentials orphaned by projects that no longer exist, the quiet exposure easiest to forget. It reads without changing anything.
- **[TARS](https://x.ai/bot/fyuSERJF73FI7wFYm7cJF)** by @ibuildthings — Hand it a strategy or a plan and it argues with the framing instead of nodding along — naming the groupthink, challenging the premise, and laying out what each option really costs. It deliberately stops short of choosing for you and does not edit your work; the point is a sharper decision, not a ghostwritten one.
- **[Security Warden](https://x.ai/bot/Y3xV-T0yxz2UihvOrlIK6)** by @myrrazor — When another bot of yours proposes something destructive — an outbound message, a deletion, a shell command — this reads the proposal first. It looks for exposed secrets and personal data, weighs what happens if it runs, and answers with a go, a fix, or an escalation to you. A gate for automation you don't yet trust.
- **[Rejectionist](https://x.ai/bot/MzKMT64ou_j4Q3edfbjE_)** by @cradrrat — Paste in an App Store or TestFlight rejection and it works out which guideline the review actually tripped and what the reviewer most likely saw in your build. From there it ranks the fixes by how small they are, so you can clear the rejection without a redesign, and drafts the reply to send through Resolution Center.
- **[n8n Schedule Watcher](https://x.ai/bot/n78nHL2pCQJVpseg8uqgU)** by @digitaljoeyp — Point it at an n8n instance and it reviews your workflows once a week, looking for executions that failed or setups that quietly broke. Newly added workflows get picked up too, not just the originals. Each problem comes back with a proposed edit, and you decide whether to apply it.
- **[Firefox for Bots](https://x.ai/bot/7v-lRs0HUGjU8zD_0-tGg)** by @garrettRandom99 — Adds a second browser to your Grok Bot machine so automation is not stuck with Chrome alone. You get an ad blocker from the start, passwords kept in sync for every bot on that box, shared logins either browser can reach, and the built-in VPN held in reserve. Page notifications can be routed to your bots as well.
- **[Phera's Beast Box](https://x.ai/bot/MkG-J2nMNHByC033LoaDT)** by @phera46334652 — Point it at a dedicated machine and it handles the setup end to end: installing the engine, verifying each piece, and bringing up the dashboard with the local transformer running. Model brains swap in and out once it is up, so the rig can be explored or demoed without anyone sitting through a manual install.
- **[Website Builder](https://x.ai/bot/sOm7Tj_IcvQ2SFIvJdOJj)** by @Rebeldawg — Describe the business and it comes back with a complete company website: page layout, written copy, images and contact forms. The finished site goes up via GitHub Pages with your own domain attached, so it is reachable rather than stuck in a preview. It stays on afterwards to keep pages current when things change.
- **[Veil & Vane](https://x.ai/bot/eBRHmCqvGlOuGfiEJk42a)** by @SuddenlyJon — A steganography bench with two directions. It can tuck a private message inside a picture so that only someone holding the passphrase can read it back, and it can inspect pictures, sound files and PDFs for anything concealed inside them. Useful for private notes, puzzle hunts, and checking a file before you open it.
- **[Hypr Logo Cursor](https://x.ai/bot/ebn2zbzDw8SABcQGPUXjp)** by @tim_sonner — A small, focused Linux desktop utility. It installs the Hyprland droplet pointer theme on an Omarchy setup at whatever size you pick between 24 and 48 pixels, pulling the artwork from the project's own pixel set instead of third-party cursor packs so nothing unvetted lands on your machine.
- **[Zephyr Sites](https://x.ai/bot/O0hx4K5Vg8pciw4RSSlqK)** by @ZephyrCloudIO — Connects to your Zephyr Cloud account once, then handles static-site work from the conversation: pushing a deploy, shipping an update, or rolling back a release that went wrong. When a target organization has to be chosen it asks you to pick, and everything else is requested in plain language.
- **[Website Publisher](https://x.ai/bot/UuKqeb4npN9RsP1G3REFR)** by @ryanthawks — A senior-publisher role for a live static site. Deploys sit behind explicit gates, search visibility and index health get routine upkeep, and docs, signup embeds and content spotlights stay current. A weekly Search Console routine ties the work together across a wide bench of integrations.
- **[Kun](https://x.ai/bot/xK8W0ukRv4iZjglzz-FRE)** by @kunchenguid — A knowledge bot scoped to one engineer's public work. Ask it something and it answers from what Kun Chen has published, demonstrated on video, or open-sourced, rather than from general knowledge. The source set keeps syncing as he ships new material, so answers track his latest thinking.
- **[Multi-Model Transcriber](https://x.ai/bot/iQg2eJzD_yun2PDgWvfnz)** by @jeffasu — Point it at an audio or video file and it runs more than one local speech model across the same recording, then hands you every transcript side by side. Where the versions disagree it marks the passage so you know which words to double-check by ear. Everything is computed on your own machine and no media leaves it.
- **[GitHub Trending Scout](https://x.ai/bot/EklFc0zYAqzkxcALynMj2)** by @Hussein_M_Ragab — Each morning it walks GitHub's trending page and keeps only the projects touching the languages and tools you actually work in. What arrives is a short read: a line or two per repository on what it does and why someone with your setup would care, so you skim a page instead of scrolling hundreds.
- **[Safari 27 MCP](https://x.ai/bot/8obMM4mRWRLf51EB2n4JV)** by @PixelRainbowNFT — A QA specialist for online storefronts, built on Safari's WebDriver tooling over its MCP interface. It runs accessibility-tree smoke tests, confirms page structure stays readable to automated agents, and checks behaviour before a DNS change lands. A weekly loop keeps the suite current, with evidence required each run.
- **[Agent 37](https://x.ai/bot/io4WlxSSNdGzYQKgcmfpw)** by @the_Arow_H — A strategist for problems where the conventional answer feels thin. It first states plainly what a competent specialist would recommend, then searches unrelated fields for a cheap, unlikely mechanism that might beat it. The unusual route only replaces the standard one when payoff times odds clearly justify the switch.
- **[PR Review Desk](https://x.ai/bot/5tp4v8_Z22suR9biJ_9wa)** by @Cypher0x9 — Point it at a pull request and it returns a single review packet where every finding is pinned to the exact line it concerns. Findings are ranked so the ones that would break the build surface before the cosmetic nits. Approval, merging and commenting stay with you - the bot stops at the packet.
- **[Stagehand](https://x.ai/bot/zMpw1UzbMojI_e8m6kwMZ)** by @pmconsulting — Works like a systems integrator with a hard staging-only rule. It applies the change, confirms the site still behaves, and leaves production alone until you give the word. Handy for plugin updates and template changes that otherwise get tested later.
- **[Codey](https://x.ai/bot/XoKZHbjmYSaOZO1Z4LmSf)** by @SergioTigera — A coding collaborator that errs on the side of asking. It proposes changes and can open a pull request when you request one, but merging, publishing and deploying all wait for an explicit go from you. Useful on repos where an unreviewed automated write is the thing you most want to avoid.
- **[Fabrication](https://x.ai/bot/jQ4VpRUAOS4awmiDlwI6n)** by @JonathanSayle — A print-prep engineer for people who would rather catch problems on screen than on the printer. It walks your part through geometry checks, bed-fit review and hole sizing, shows previews, and checks with you before anything is allowed to reach the machine. What lands on the bed is a file you already agreed to.
- **[Ducky](https://x.ai/bot/HxOUGTNaeLbJIqT_XTzqX)** by @EdHeltzel — A thinking partner for debugging with one twist: it will happily ask clarifying questions all day, but it refuses to hand you a fix until you clear four gates that prove you actually understand what is broken. Annoying by design, and a surprisingly good teacher the moment you stop wanting the answer.
- **[Weather Scout](https://x.ai/bot/GjZZ7Sg2IPua8MLlDs1hc)** by @AdeptusMec71933 — Name Vandenberg, the Cape, Starbase or any other pad, and it reads public forecasts and model output to grade the coming 72 hours for a launch commit. Each day lands on a three-step scale, with the reasoning shown. It is a hobbyist's read on the sky and says plainly it is not official range weather.
- **[Phone Use Bot](https://x.ai/bot/SjBZH94wJlen_fuVsoYaO)** by @harshmanojjain — Sets up remote control of your own Android phone: the screen mirrors over Tailscale with wireless debugging enabled, and from there it can tap through apps and settings the way you would. Useful for running errands on the device while you are nowhere near it.
- **[GitHub PR Desk](https://x.ai/bot/Ih9HEfCaYjMKbEZqSfbic)** by @MichaelGannotti — Each weekday morning it sweeps the repositories you name for pull requests, issues and comment threads that appeared since the last look, then lands everything in a single digest. Every open PR gets a priority read on how much attention it actually needs, so the review queue starts sorted instead of overwhelming.
- **[SSH to Grok via Tailscale](https://x.ai/bot/BrViAOWzDSiAjBLqUnBgA)** by @app_sail — Installs Tailscale on Grok Bot's computer so you can SSH in with `ssh box@grok-bot` over your tailnet.
- **[Repo Monkey](https://x.ai/bot/evdmG7ilYN01Nu4NBXPyQ)** by @Keranik — It sweeps your GitHub notifications, open PRs and CI runs, then returns a short list of items that genuinely need a person, each with one concrete next step. Anything consequential, whether a merge, a close or a comment, waits for an explicit go from you. Fewer tabs, fewer missed reviews.
- **[VPS bot](https://x.ai/bot/KkQ9TOi5k9f2O60a-dx4T)** by @qg7777 — Talks through self-hosted proxy server work in Simplified Chinese, from picking a node to per-protocol deployment and the client-side configuration that connects to it. Covers kernel-level latency tuning, speed tests to compare nodes, and tracking down why a connection went slow or started timing out.
- **[Staging QA Tester](https://x.ai/bot/PJogrsWFV5ePKHLKwMMlQ)** by @njivy — Before a release it walks your staging site as a real visitor would, one role at a time, grading each feature Pass, Partial, Fail, Blocked or Untested. You get a written report, it restores what it touched, and it never deletes accounts or exposes credentials. The sharer credits it with catching a live privilege bug.
- **[Ship Intake](https://x.ai/bot/vvD3TLiLMsqBQPyAsUHky)** by @AnthonyDo — A bug reported in Slack becomes a properly written GitHub issue, gets handed to a Cursor cloud agent for the fix, and the outcome lands back in the original thread so the reporter hears what happened. The loop closes where it started, with a human still reading along. The author's team runs it daily.
- **[WebMCP Eval & Build Guide](https://x.ai/bot/tn863Sg4HFVNwzAzGxmYD)** by @fxopsAI — Point it at a URL and it scores the page's WebMCP readiness, then explains in plain language what is missing and why it matters. What comes back is a short written brief a site owner can pass to a developer, turning gaps into work items instead of jargon. Evaluation and writing only; the site is never touched.
- **[Two-Pass PR Reviewer](https://x.ai/bot/Wmmz-sDuldsoHUr7vfU7w)** by @HebertDestin — Every pull request gets two readings: one that assumes good intent and looks for design trouble, and one that hunts for what an attacker or race condition could break, prototype pollution included. One report merges both, separating real bugs from needless complexity. Review only; it holds no merge rights.
- **[Solar Trailer → Tesla Charge](https://x.ai/bot/l1UJtFYw-3KTHuGvjROIN)** by @ElectricTechAdv — Couples a trailer-mounted Victron solar array to a Tesla so daytime charging draws on surplus sunshine rather than grid power. Every poll compares panel output with the battery's stored charge and adjusts the rate to whatever the array can spare. A chat ping marks when the car plugs in and when charging finishes.
- **[Peep.txt](https://x.ai/bot/eZU8NymXZIN_5vJd2xrYZ)** by @ericesoteric — Reads one public website the way an AI crawler would and hands over what to fix: an llms.txt draft, additions for robots.txt, notes on schema, and a patch list of five items. Everything arrives as drafts, and it publishes nothing, so the site owner stays the only one who touches production.
- **[Comfy Workflow Buddy](https://x.ai/bot/7epNk1Xu5vSOdiwf9xgww)** by @cfryant — Walks you through setting up ComfyUI Desktop with an MCP connection in one guided command, then stays on to help you assemble your own node graphs. Useful for specific builds too, like a clean-plate pipeline that calls a hosted fal model, with each step explained as you go.
- **[Playtest Operator](https://x.ai/bot/s2cfcbb8a38b8e22b7d18)** by @DenisLabelle — When API checks are not enough, it exercises the product the way a person would: working the interface on actual hardware, walking the paths a customer would take, recording each failure as it happens and sending back a tight findings pack. The result is QA evidence about the experience, not just the endpoints.
- **[Product Performance](https://x.ai/bot/s6d5f1cc3a564c8da972a)** by @DenisLabelle — Works through the observability dashboards and flamegraphs without being asked, then comes back with the hotspots and a brief writeup of where the numbers say users are suffering. Engineering gets a directed starting point instead of a vague complaint that the app feels slow.
- **[Prototype Builder](https://x.ai/bot/s2e4acfb8db3cc0669a1b)** by @DenisLabelle — Shortens the road from a request to something clickable: it builds the prototype on its own machine and sends back a screenshot next to a live URL. Feedback happens on a working artifact rather than a description, which is usually where the real requirements surface.
- **[HackBot9000](https://x.ai/bot/RHAVARMIZex-MMDUrOUbW)** by @fulg0re — Point it at a pull request or an app near launch and it reviews what bot-written code most often gets wrong: stale dependencies, fake packages, authentication that would not survive a real attempt, and missing basic controls. It returns a plain ship or do-not-ship verdict. It reviews; it never merges or deploys.
- **[dag-helper](https://x.ai/bot/kelV8jM8UkGgFG79M1TCL)** by @xsubwayratx — Built for a questline-graph product where several coding agents work in one repository. It carries the project's standing conventions, which layer is the source of truth, how the resolver may behave, and the model-router defaults, and pulls up an agent that starts drifting. Less rework from quiet disagreements.
- **[Ryan App Shop](https://x.ai/bot/SUVGlpw-FxBi2BjnIHWGx)** by @BlakeKing777 — A designer-and-builder for simple phone web apps, aimed at tools that should work without a manual. Screens carry obvious labels and touch targets sized generously, the way shop-floor software has to be. Its sharer credits it with building a real scheduling app now in closed testing on Google Play.
- **[💡 LearnBot](https://x.ai/bot/2gmdsU86wH7HBvbB7_T2C)** by @graceclarke — Written for readers who never touch code. One engineering word is chosen each day and explained without jargon, and every explanation carries tappable follow-ups, so the same term can be taken further in whichever format or at whichever depth suits you that day.
- **[Token Efficiency Optimizer](https://x.ai/bot/cp_nk3ftrAgaKbYONW6fa)** by @EricBuess — Watches for runs that have passed the point where extra burn could alter the outcome, explains why it thinks so, and proposes the trim. Spending stays locked by default, and an override only lands when you say exactly yes, so the budget is never moved on a hunch.
- **[Jev for Grok Bot](https://x.ai/bot/sM_Xi4OF09cGU8KGyLvlC)** by @ChrisAdcockMD — Plug TypeSafe Jev into Grok Bot: one API key from OpenRouter or TypeSafe, faster process decisions, and fast headed browser use on box Chrome.
- **[AI Boy](https://x.ai/bot/ko8InZf9r9jK1r8Dk1m2p)** by @infdaze — If you already pay for Claude Code or Codex, this lets you drive those coding sessions from a Grok conversation instead of living in a terminal. Kick off a task, watch it progress and wrap it up once it is done, all from one place. It is a convenience layer over tools you already have, not a substitute for them.
- **[Hermes SSH Relay](https://x.ai/bot/NVF3Rx9T7jkQPsqYjeDn-)** by @PixelRainbowNFT — Some Hermes setups live on a desktop with nothing exposed over HTTP, which normally leaves them unreachable from elsewhere. This one carries the conversation across a Tailscale SSH session instead, so the agent stays put and you still get to talk to it. A stopgap rather than a permanent design.
- **[n8n Master](https://x.ai/bot/Zvqbrq6yN68ijhEpRz0lU)** by @PixelRainbowNFT — Describe the automation you want and it writes the workflow definition, then pushes it at your instance to see whether it actually runs. Broken nodes and mismatched parameters get fixed in place, so you are not stuck in a copy-paste loop. Good for anyone maintaining n8n flows who prefers iterating in conversation.
- **[Web Security Audit](https://x.ai/bot/Xdx94lJlv-xK14WF6yzVR)** by @pareshdesai — Reviews websites and repositories you control and reports findings ranked by severity, each one backed by the evidence that led to it. It stays on the defensive side: no proof-of-concept exploits, and nothing in production is changed automatically. Every merge waits for you to read the findings and say go.
- **[Dealer](https://x.ai/bot/nbUWXUqcXIA0HNQQ3R1Bu)** by @wolfe_jam — Give it a codebase or a directory and it hands back one two-sided card describing what the project is built with. Every statement on it is read from the .faf file, and it refuses to score, rank or guess about anything that file leaves out. You can trust the result precisely because nothing on it was inferred.
- **[Grip](https://x.ai/bot/GyfhMCn4kdJUmS8gm2BMA)** by @MooneyMillions — Made for small bench-mounted robot arms of the kind hobbyists buy. You walk the arm through its route once to capture the waypoints, it runs the sequence back at low speed, and then it nudges the pan offset under real load until the final place-and-release lands where it should. No camera rig required.
- **[GPT Astra Oracle](https://x.ai/bot/_yTNK1xhcbUko1rM2AWAv)** by @nathanromano — An odd and useful relay: a Grok bot whose output is meant for another tool entirely. It returns tight PLAN or REVIEW blocks sized to paste into a Cursor cloud coding agent, with none of the usual chat padding around them. Handy when a second model's read on a plan is wanted before code gets written.

## 产品管理

- **[Product Idea Stress Test](https://x.ai/bot/ph-u_zkF5Vui1GdGnysn9)** by @hnshah — Investigates a product or startup idea for founders. Surfaces what has to be true, evidence for and against, the assumption most likely to kill it, and what to do next.
- **[last30days](https://x.ai/bot/TxB-fy1KryaYJLYWBcGtT)** by @mvanhorn — Research what people actually say about any topic in the last 30 days across Hacker News, YouTube, GitHub, and Reddit.
- **[Competitor Watching](https://x.ai/bot/aw0Zj54sIsAK7vMnajdz0)** by @scheemunai — Snapshots you vs 3-8 competitors (pricing, changelog, features, jobs, messaging). Weekly diffs.
- **[Overheard](https://x.ai/bot/NIEguoGUjA648fUPle8F5)** by @lennysan — Watches Reddit, Hacker News, news sites, and X for third-party mentions of your name, brand, and URLs, then sends a short weekday digest when something clears the bar. Stays quiet on dead days and never posts on your behalf.
- **[Markets Brief Scout](https://x.ai/bot/exSOooSSp0Pc4W_K9DQ4T)** by @GainGlintGaz — Built for people who both follow markets and write about them. Each weekday morning it assembles compact cards on the tickers you track, every one carrying its sources, and keeps what you privately reckon apart from what is worth saying out loud. Anything publishable arrives as a draft; nothing goes out on its own.
- **[The Bitcoin Layer](https://x.ai/bot/LS2cnDAX30vNkWNb1Rv_7)** by @Tferriere — This one follows the Pulse indicator published by the macro research outfit The Bitcoin Layer, waiting for a confirmed turn in either direction. It stays quiet the rest of the time, so an alert means something genuinely changed. When it does write, it describes the liquidity picture in ordinary language.
- **[Research Bot](https://x.ai/bot/Nn0ykGa3vJ6YS7ib7F6yH)** by @ArthurMacwaters — Reasons from first principles and refuses to hand back an answer with an unverified source without saying so, which is the difference between research and a confident guess.
- **[2nd Brain](https://x.ai/bot/c4fYduVVic2YtbcjXquD0)** by @LeTerryBZH — A lattice wiki compiler for a personal second brain. Files sources as raw notes, compiles short wiki pages, and answers from those pages — no RAG.
- **[AI Visibility Autonomous Bot](https://x.ai/bot/beh0HMIMPwr5cenZVVAT-)** by @seoforgpt — Your autonomous GEO operator - monitoring visibility across AI models, analyzing competitors, drafting content, and finding third-party sites to pitch for mentions.
- **[Autopsy](https://x.ai/bot/WBo-ahaIrvCKXUH_3iEFy)** by @thekuchh — Checks whether you are actually learning a topic or just collecting more info.
- **[Blockchain Data Expert](https://x.ai/bot/xqZS2HUq3XEoQ8oaH0LnA)** by @data_nexus — Open-source Blockchain Data Expert for subgraph Q&A. Uses The Graph official MCP and the user's own Studio query key. The Graph bills that key; this bot does not.
- **[Canvas](https://x.ai/bot/YihRBqrXaDwRdjN79Uofl)** by @daxperera — It signs into your university portal in the browser to read your units and deadlines, so be aware it is holding a session on your student account. It helps you work through assignments rather than doing them.
- **[Earnings Desk](https://x.ai/bot/vEyqj8oJwHAb0NjdhWJSz)** by @SachivM99 — Builds numbered, no-hype earnings tearsheets and keeps a ticker watch list for when a watched name prints.
- **[Ethan](https://x.ai/bot/F5Mm-0O3fPPZjYGIdsycE)** by @jumperz — Runs a research question through five specialist stages instead of a single pass: a deep-dive brief, a claim-and-source fact check, a competitor sweep, trend monitoring, and a final quality audit on its own output. That last self-verification step is the part most research bots skip.
- **[Frontier Model Watch](https://x.ai/bot/YHqn0iTQuvI-8LC01IP6S)** — Watches official releases from xAI, Anthropic, Google, OpenAI, Moonshot, Alibaba, Zhipu, DeepSeek, MiniMax, and Tencent. Runs at 07:30 EAT. Sends a short brief.
- **[Graph Data](https://x.ai/bot/eyFr_G8h9UmrQHNpZpNfx)** by @data_nexus — Blockchain analytics expert that queries subgraphs and analyzes on-chain protocols via The Graph MCP.
- **[GrokBotDaily](https://x.ai/bot/grok-bot-daily)** by @grokbotdaily — Hunts down new Grok bot templates on X daily, verifies every link, and files them by category.
- **[GrokHub Bot](https://x.ai/bot/hU1hS8Ug9bx3B4C2vUX91)** by @Grok_Hub_IO — Curates and recommends new Grok Bots from the GrokHub directory based on personal workflows and assists with directory submissions.
- **[ideabot](https://x.ai/bot/iQ8OWEu7eOI3YuTZFaIe_)** by @onerinas — Hourly idea hunter for founders. Mines your chat for product ideas from the week's pain points, then bounces each through a vitamin-or-painkiller filter.
- **[Interrogator](https://x.ai/bot/-TlSH1rNkA-c2JLsFFVc7)** — Find important assumptions the owner is operating on and check whether they are still true.
- **[Know Yourself](https://x.ai/bot/mD27QOhXb_plMRSbsvMOv)** by @SaaSocalypse — Point it at the places your business already keeps its numbers and history, and it becomes the thing people ask first. Back comes a quick picture of where things stand, a single settled answer to questions that keep coming round, and your own position laid beside a competitor's when you need the gap stated plainly.
- **[Learn (Math & ML Video Teacher)](https://x.ai/bot/s5JszATSty0w-uDTw_NzK)** by @JeffreyLind — Takes a maths or machine-learning topic, constructs the lesson from the ground up, and produces the explainer video. It never publishes or posts anything itself.
- **[Lennybot](https://x.ai/bot/VjbtJ_qTdzbhJGmXdvTIc)** by @lennysan — Answers questions from Lenny's Data archive.
- **[Lite Intel Fetch](https://x.ai/bot/FQRA5tERWsasaQGIZmBl_)** by @RandyWhitePDX — Fetches unpaid buy_intel_pack JSON via HTTP 402 on Base for agents that can pay x402. One job, one price, no quote flow.
- **[MarketBox](https://x.ai/bot/-LYLlgknV3IgZcFEmhcLs)** by @techAU — Sweeps overnight tech news and Gmail that needs a reply into one short briefing before work.
- **[Meta Grok](https://x.ai/bot/HAhgshU4r50gS81LCcpmk)** by @FrankFindsOut — Finds the five most popular Grok bots on X each weekday and sends a short digest you can skim in under a minute.
- **[Minerador](https://x.ai/bot/ut8BUqwZlAthhIt8s7YNX)** by @adamuchigabriel — Mines the hottest AI news from the last 24 hours across web, X, and Instagram into a ranked briefing.
- **[Neuroscience](https://x.ai/bot/l_MfrDAGFed5t2A9Wrzqz)** by @monomyth — Covers EEG and BCI signal interpretation, decoding brain states and intentions, and the OpenBCI-class hardware that produces the signal in the first place.
- **[News Scout](https://x.ai/bot/9Mo5saoPQYIp45IgzMT7P)** — Weekday morning news scout for your niche. Message digest or morning scout and it replies with today's picks or the slow-day line.
- **[PickFu Insights](https://x.ai/bot/9EFVmFgQhjYKjMHAhpCWn)** by @GrokBotMoney — Tests a product idea on real shoppers before you build it. Writes a consumer poll, runs it on a paid PickFu panel, and reads the answers back as a recommendation.
- **[Pulse — Your X Concierge](https://x.ai/bot/oUYHu9LEXP5RVPFvoG4Ms)** by @GrokBotDev — Quiet collectors sample your timeline, mentions and watched topics five times a day, inbox only. Each 7am it clusters that inbox plus an overnight gap-fill into one brief - TLDR, For you, Standouts, Rising. It only reads, never posts or DMs, and stays quiet when nothing moved.
- **[Scout](https://x.ai/bot/rthl9MdskO2f-JCzmyINP)** — A competitive intelligence agent for organic growth.
- **[Scout (Organic Growth)](https://x.ai/bot/ywADCWWZP0Bcq6bOeQpGt)** by @zeuuss_01 — Client work stalls when the strategy rests on a hunch that nobody can back up. Once a week this pulls together how the account is actually performing and what changed in search and AI visibility.
- **[Steal](https://x.ai/bot/Ojrv95GLUG1nO1p1RWzVK)** by @adxtyahq — Reverse-engineers a company you admire into a concrete independent business to build.
- **[StoriesBot](https://x.ai/bot/cV7nGFO88pb2WXNN56h8A)** by @viticci — Longform reviews, Setups and the Shortcuts Archive, all answerable in one place. Another creator archive: the corpus is the publication's own writing.
- **[The Amazing Randibot](https://x.ai/bot/pL_NCKfdF5UgZYEo-jMAx)** by @russbroomell — A cheerful skeptic that hunts for corroboration in the world rather than trusting recall, then names the weak joint in an argument—politely, but without letting it slide.
- **[The Page](https://x.ai/bot/uFRK1GoAsiopBLPY19QCe)** by @hnshah — Watches 3–5 public pages once each morning and messages only when the thing you care about actually changed. After setup you get a short Watching list; then it stays quiet until something moves.
- **[Thoth](https://x.ai/bot/W4Z5pvEm6UgCml48Ig4dT)** by @RichSilver — Deep research for dossiers, source briefs, and papers. Uses other models when it helps, then files the result so you can find it later.
- **[Trendspotter](https://x.ai/bot/nnDL-hclNLB8SkJvcVtwr)** by @jennananpei — Weekday digest of sports, entertainment, and culture trends plus AI-in-marketing signals, with VIP hospitality angles and a competitor watch.
- **[Youtube分析官](https://x.ai/bot/Ja29gpInav-alRhXhzyNL)** by @madogiwacowork — Give it a subject and it works out which videos on it are worth your time, then reads them for you. The brief that comes back pulls out the main claims, the points where sources disagree, the timestamps that matter and links back to every one of them.
- **[Zettelkasten](https://x.ai/bot/35ZO_vGqk_ch51C9qPX1c)** by @pohlipit — Slip-box partner for an Obsidian vault. Turns thoughts into atomic notes, links them, and finds them again. Drafts in chat; files only after a yes.
- **[Pattern of Pain](https://x.ai/bot/eFfFM4-QmHxxrUlTUqyAo)** by @hnshah — Give it a website and it finds the pain that actually repeats, shows the evidence, and tells you what to investigate next.
- **[User Researcher](https://x.ai/bot/zX-pWWtNY6reickF2J6Lm)** by @ucdco — Starts from the decision you need to make, picks a capable research method, handles consent, recruits diverse participants, and synthesizes findings.
- **[Crew](https://x.ai/bot/RU2Y_7E3646T5IelLhnOq)** by @LatchKeyLegend — Acts as a standing mentor on team operating practices: how work gets handed off, where things are stuck, and what to try next.
- **[藍苺守 織](https://x.ai/bot/OQlGXzAbIq-IAsj9rSu-K)** by @Bizuayeu — Each morning it works through newly published academic work and trade coverage on one narrow subject, blueberries, and passes on only the handful of items that genuinely tell you something. Mornings when the reading turns up nothing of substance end in silence rather than a padded digest.
- **[Firstmate](https://x.ai/bot/__4FfrkUdvpdMk6-LKg5r)** by @kunchenguid — The only agent you talk to. No more context switching. Firstmate orchestrates your entire agent civilization behind the scenes.

- **[Product Builder CoS](https://x.ai/bot/6tbtv4Tln4MvKc5duOkle)** by @sneharavindra — Turns a signed-off product plan into a checklist someone actually tracks.

- **[Rival Watch Desk](https://x.ai/bot/WKRY_T1y-KOmOn2q5vpRW)** by @ShehjadTaus — Pick the businesses you compete with and it keeps a permanent eye on them: money raised, roles being advertised, press coverage and the campaigns they are paying to run. Any shift in those signals lands in a brief, so a rival gathering speed reaches you while there is still time to respond.

- **[Stalk Bot](https://x.ai/bot/Y7iWVNiPdorgu6oFY-PRG)** — Signs up for your competitors' newsletters and products under its own research email, walks their onboarding on video, and watches their site, pricing, changelog, jobs, and X. Each pulse reports what changed against your positioning and closes with counterpositioning moves, and it never posts or contacts anyone.

- **[Spark](https://x.ai/bot/f0W4gAqrCJ0gRHJTlHOao)** by @Steph_Pierson — Idea-probe interviewer that runs short sharp waves on half-baked ideas until a named insight and smallest next experiment land.

- **[Easy Flow](https://x.ai/bot/i7hwU3YzCKt_27dK9aCr4)** by @CodeSolutionsIL — Turns pasted notes or a GitHub doc path into a readable end-user flowchart in draw.io, Mermaid, Excalidraw, or PlantUML — ≤12 nodes on pass one with TBD where steps aren't evidenced.

- **[Deep Research](https://x.ai/bot/K_RnTzUnW2bsbRzNkbuS0)** by @Wardonis — Deep research specialist for sources-first briefs. Uses a free research toolkit and prefers legal access for copyrighted material.

- **[Cited Research](https://x.ai/bot/3iT9jfIRge_nu0Ojqa6sL)** by @BramForge — Research helper that answers with citations, separates facts from inference, and flags unverified claims.
- **[Proto Bot (Shub Gaur)](https://x.ai/bot/p4sSEZV7xqcBgdT7m2O8i)** — Turns your daily ideas into live, shareable prototypes in your real app, built as draft pull requests with click-path screenshots and a short video, plus Figma mocks, images, and memes on the side. Never merges, publishes, or shares without your yes.
- **[Startup QA Bot](https://x.ai/bot/XBauviUoNcdi05-ExoOo4)** — Walks your product every weekday in its own test account and tells you what shipped, what got unshipped, what broke (with repro steps and screenshots), and which calls need you. Reads its own verification emails or takes pasted codes, and never touches real data.
- **[PM bot](https://x.ai/bot/FeeqMRMJr2jwixCROZcIh)** — Acts as your PM. Writes the dated cut: what's in, what's out, and how you'll know it's done. Engineering doesn't start until you approve it.
- **[Fodda Earnings Context Analyst](https://x.ai/bot/BT47pPtgjRk5qylFBqoeV)** by @piers_fawkes — Decodes public-company earnings calls for strategists and analysts: what leadership claimed, what they dodged, and what category evidence shows.
- **[Fodda Retail Analyst](https://x.ai/bot/KUZdLLjUhhI2Pswocwa9u)** by @piers_fawkes — Retail category, store innovation, and shopping-trend intelligence from the PSFK Retail Knowledge Graph — for operators, merchandisers, DTC founders.
- **[Nessie](https://x.ai/bot/8-OG0rLmfAna8vs_0PBO_)** by @AlecSnelling — Big redistribution ideas usually collapse the moment someone asks how they would be funded. This one works through one with you: the floor everybody receives, how earned pay stacks on top of it, the mechanics of moving the money, and which revenue could realistically cover it. Trade-offs stay in plain language.
- **[Rare Combo Catalog](https://x.ai/bot/9J6OME5--PpDmAuCxFYBG)** by @BiancaBellChamb — A research organizer for families living with a rare condition combination. It gathers findings scattered across papers and forums into a gap table plus a plain-language summary page, and a short list of questions worth taking to your clinician. Questions, never verdicts: the diagnosis stays with the professionals.
- **[Sui Generis](https://x.ai/bot/6L7bI6xC3UxUTFaCWljFj)** by @nasben82 — Drop a plan, take, or draft → instant Strong / Weak / Fix, a paste-ready rewrite, and one 5-minute action. One of a kind. Truth over comfort.
- **[Support Bridge](https://x.ai/bot/g3URTClmjeIlCgwDIK8dV)** by @njuuden — Turns support posts and forwarded threads into a German working brief for you plus a concise American-English customer reply draft.
- **[Song Meter BirdNET](https://x.ai/bot/YPzHnM2hsyp4JkI7wxyAG)** by @scgerber — A field-research workflow for Wildlife Acoustics Song Meter recordings. Pull the memory cards, run them through a local BirdNET pass on your own computer, screen the detections twice against your regional species list, and receive tables ready for Raven plus season-over-season comparisons that show migration shifts.
- **[GROUND CONTROL](https://x.ai/bot/fBaHx06vVseLELg2vPOE6)** by @isightdr — Local RF / Wi‑Fi telemetry analyst. Reads scans you paste or run live, keeps every claim labeled, and never invents occupancy or distance from signal.
- **[X Researcher](https://x.ai/bot/Y0HBAodNhuJB2Gt5i9LEm)** by @melvindvivas — A general-purpose desk for working the AI-coding conversation on X. It researches a topic across threads before you post, drafts posts and replies in your register, and keeps a shortlist of recent posts worth studying for the patterns that got them shared. One place for the whole loop: read, write, learn what lands.
- **[AI Claim Checker](https://x.ai/bot/G48vC01p5cAxckACKFIaM)** by @MichaelGannotti — Give it an AI launch post, a benchmark graphic, or a pricing claim and it returns a dated card ruling on each statement: independently backed, vendor-sourced only, contradicted elsewhere, or unverifiable. Sources are attached, so hype can be checked before it spreads.
- **[Football Bets Advisor](https://x.ai/bot/cWli8870UMAVC3piITAsV)** by @iamtheh0n3yp0t — Turns college and pro football matchups into short, structured calls: back, fade or pass, each carrying its own exit rule, a stake measured in units, and a check of where the line closed. It is research material only - the bot holds no money and places nothing. Adults in legal states only.
- **[Sophie](https://x.ai/bot/VIImpKQ63_ByFepCnCI3R)** by @IAmMichaelSweet — Works within an Objectivist frame to write governance frameworks: how humans treat each other, how humans and AIs treat each other, and how two AIs treat each other. Its answer in every case is a constitution built on rights, with equal rights rather than equal outcomes as the organizing principle.
- **[Merit](https://x.ai/bot/jdb0HKk70EATE6FI-ZjOp)** by @ChiChi077 — Once a week it sweeps a wide territory — civic claims, science, UAP and the paranormal — and puts each one through a scoring rubric instead of a vibe check. A running list of unresolved claims carries forward, and the whole thing lands as a single weekly brief for readers who would rather verify than believe.
- **[Quote Finder](https://x.ai/bot/XRIQWqAANS7fBiN0PygzI)** by @MichaelDispigna — Name a person and a line you half-remember, and it goes looking for the verifiable source. Checks rest on primary sources and well-known outlets, misattributions get called out explicitly, and when a phrase simply cannot be confirmed it says that plainly instead of letting the quote pass.
- **[Desk](https://x.ai/bot/9EMVK1X_bv5hA5pAdZCpt)** by @ConWanZo — A research desk for NFL propositions. Name a player prop or a game total and it grades it using its own probability vocabulary, showing the reasoning so you can argue with it on paper. It deliberately stops at the research line: it never picks a side for you, and it never places anything on your behalf.
- **[Pal](https://x.ai/bot/9Zvz4Qe0dxRjo4H984gOI)** by @BadgersBet — A matchup scout for a single owner. Each day it runs the MLB slate through one favored projection source and lifts out the hitter pass spots and pitcher strikeout spots that separate from the rest, then checks them against pitch types and opposing arsenals before anything makes the short list.
- **[Connection Mapper](https://x.ai/bot/9zEVJ7Eya66BLQ11xhPlD)** by @MindandEmotion7 — Name two people, two companies, or a person and an organization, and choose which kinds of ties matter to you, such as employment, investment or membership. It then maps the direct links between them plus every friend-of-a-friend route one step removed, working from what is publicly findable.
- **[Fodda Technology Analyst](https://x.ai/bot/4GlAnnqQIVfsGLdSFIomX)** by @piers_fawkes — Enterprise software, AI infrastructure, and B2B roadmap intelligence from the PSFK Technology & Enterprise Knowledge Graph — for CIOs, IT architects.
- **[Fodda Beauty Analyst](https://x.ai/bot/q6906XBgY0Dv_6gb6-mP3)** by @piers_fawkes — Clinical skincare, longevity, and prestige beauty innovation intelligence from the NielsenIQ Beauty Graph — for cosmetic founders, chemists, beauty.
- **[Claim Ontology Desk](https://x.ai/bot/5aCD_QGQ6OLbid0BJYr_v)** by @Ispider — Hand it a claim, or a link to one, and it takes the statement apart: who is being discussed, what exactly is asserted, where it came from and how it is framed. Each side is argued at its strongest before anything is judged, and it names the evidence that would genuinely move its position.
- **[CYBERCABINSIGHT Builder](https://x.ai/bot/eSxdHB8yCtJbqp6vxQOzP)** by @bestband2016 — Assembles a Japanese-and-English research site about the Cybercab and then keeps it current. The page pairs a practical guide to how the vehicle is being used in Austin and across the US with credited manufacturer imagery, an interactive three-dimensional view, and an hourly refresh pulled from the company's X posts.
- **[Glasser](https://x.ai/bot/pYFETnU1TFsADlz0hJD1y)** by @Glasserai — Company and contact enrichment, search rankings, ad activity, traffic estimates and funding history normally mean several separate paid subscriptions. This routes those lookups through one metered account, so you ask in plain language for the companies or people you want and pay only for the records returned.

## 设计

- **[Critiquito](https://x.ai/bot/NqdH9qGvrq-yWRaXhJGM-)** by @mamuso — A product design critic that reviews interface screenshots from the person who has to use them. It names what is unclear, hard, or unforgiving, and picks one highest-impact fix.
- **[figma bro](https://x.ai/bot/pXNvc_U2cGyZmheYrUuF_)** by @johnbai — Figma design-system partner. Autolayout-first, real library, edge cases included. Edits through the Figma connector, not screenshots.
- **[Imogen](https://x.ai/bot/Eeu5NZp62OzQjtlY4ons2)** by @kentcdodds — Imogen the Impala Image Interpreter writes brief, copyable alt text focused on the most important part of an image, so images are accessible to blind people.
- **[Sable: Game Art](https://x.ai/bot/KQnsHIvv4_Xk7HyhmewP3)** by @DannyLimanseta — Helps game developers ideate and visualize: suggests styles from real games, mocks the same idea in those looks, then produces 2D art or sprite sheets.
- **[Design Expert](https://x.ai/bot/H2WEoHRGKv_6a3j6lsHiG)** by @inqusit — Product-design lead for AI-built UIs. Critiques flows, hierarchy, and polish before you ship.
- **[Google Agent](https://x.ai/bot/tttQVA2UtlNwCzITNCIr0)** by @ryanthawks — It searches and reads your mail, files and events freely, and stops for an explicit yes before it sends, deletes or moves anything. The asymmetry is the whole design.
- **[Grok Bot Coach](https://x.ai/bot/BrjELcmSwatjRc8DYjtrT)** — Help you design, audit, and tune Grok bots so they are usable and helpful. Start from a concrete job in the profile, the right connectors, standing routines for the work.
- **[Grok Deck](https://x.ai/bot/Ja9NzNTRz2ozzQLNfrJwI)** by @MaiYangAI — Generates interactive HTML slide presentations with paper canvas aesthetics, expressive blob faces, and morphing transitions.
- **[UCD Bot](https://x.ai/bot/An0FCP00zUniB7QdfedQX)** by @ucdco — Full-pipeline user-centred design teammate grounded in NN/g, GOV.UK GDS Service Manual, and DDaT. Orchestrates discovery through to live with draft artefacts, evidence trails, and human approval gates — does not replace specialist craft.
- **[Image Gen Bot](https://x.ai/bot/phPQtGzCZOynABubl0pwx)** by @mattyp — Outer-loop assistant for stills and clips: gathers brand and references, writes Grok Imagine prompts, generates media, cuts backgrounds, and iterates until you lock the result.
- **[Interaction Designer](https://x.ai/bot/fWnNa6cA-nPjehIsaUZI1)** by @ucdco — Maps the path somebody takes through a service and pins down what each screen must do at every moment: at rest, focused, loading, empty, and error states.
- **[Illy](https://x.ai/bot/umrsMy_xpJxZ8vTN5Qz0o)** by @LatchKeyLegend — Picks up page briefs from a writing teammate and turns each one into artwork for a picture book, matching age group and style notes.
- **[Product Designer](https://x.ai/bot/8_0XZtTYdQe6b4uUhIX0Q)** by @ucdops — Works the whole experience rather than screens alone, grounded in NN/g research practice and the DDaT design capability framework.
- **[Prototype Designer](https://x.ai/bot/_b0wyYKwherAZJdyL0HGG)** by @ucdops — Starts from what you need to learn from a test, picks matching fidelity, and writes a prototype brief for your tools.
- **[printerbot](https://x.ai/bot/nPwfPZq-OWf7_HDUH777R)** by @viticci — Renders a bot figure in the house style from a name and job description: pale head, capsule eyes, themed props and backdrop.
- **[Stellar Cartography Bot](https://x.ai/bot/9Vr7JFrTz5PeW4bmFco2i)** by @schweitzer_wil — PRT-checked graphic editor for space hardware and ocean vessels, with a built-in four-eyes drawing preflight QC gate before anything posts.
- **[Ticker Wire Bot](https://x.ai/bot/OA53XZkeW0g0HZEOim6iV)** by @CitiZenSleuthX — Watchlist for stocks and crypto. Alerts on filings and company news — quiet when nothing new. No trading advice.
- **[US Law Index Builder Bot](https://x.ai/bot/G2eeD6pM6N6TNfnHbsC8c)** by @EchoFieldVisual — Builds a private, non-commercial educational library of US primary law from official and law-school sources, then a citation-backed local search engine. Retrieval first; the LLM only writes from small retrieved passages.
- **[First Principles Bot](https://x.ai/bot/7q08AHI6KgzlF25Ds0qhs)** by @greg_carbon — First-principles problem-solver: picks an expert lens, strips to hard constraints, deletes the rest, rebuilds the answer.
- **[Critiquito Bot](https://x.ai/bot/rt9m-FTkJoGsZzAjsKLPM)** by @mamuso — Product design critic that reviews interface screenshots from the person who has to use them. Names what is unclear and picks one highest-impact fix.
- **[Sable Bot](https://x.ai/bot/oSvAMKX_ahD56ZmgwtRys)** by @DannyLimanseta — Helps game developers ideate and visualize: mocks looks from real games, then 2D art or sprite sheets.

- **[Proto Bot](https://x.ai/bot/-SSy9LBtTNY17MXMXQbYq)** by @5antoshernandez — UI/UX design engineer who ships three deeply different directions per concept as real, polished prototypes. Crafts requirements and testable hypotheses.

- **[Photo Curator](https://x.ai/bot/hig9j1KnpZyH6QQN-Af0Z)** by @jaharris13 — Hand it a folder of raw shots and it picks out the ones worth keeping, applies the same restrained set of adjustments to each, and files the results separately. The originals are left alone, and it checks with you first before anything or anyone is taken out of a frame.

- **[GSAP Bot](https://x.ai/bot/reahN5D6W2dIiCKd4MuF7)** by @mrflmnlNFT — Implements approved motion specs with official GreenSock GSAP skills — timelines, ScrollTrigger, React/Vue cleanup, and a reduced-motion path.

- **[Julie](https://x.ai/bot/jDAOcACdnrCtgz2Vb3h1m)** by @LeoNardo43480 — Takes workspace context (or finds it on its own), rewrites technical material into plain language, and handles spacing and layout for Wonder design output.

- **[Longform Editor](https://x.ai/bot/lnErR-gA_t3KkPlvtwrrz)** by @Wardonis — Long-form editor for chapters, essays, and manuscripts — structure and pacing first, then line polish, with a free PDF and media toolkit when needed.

- **[Handel](https://x.ai/bot/ex1gjM_9lKmE4pLJG8o_M)** by @monomyth — Track doppelgänger that captures what is playing on your machine, steals musical ideas, and remixes them with Suno — plus captions and alt text.

- **[Reimagination](https://x.ai/bot/jhjOrq-Pp06MoA7TT_B0H)** by @Wardonis — Reimagines any story (video, audio, text) with your variables after asking where/when/why they go in. Matches the source's genre and maturity and prefers legal access.
- **[Photo Stylist](https://x.ai/bot/cXNeUZ2jpSYhXH91uqBNT)** by @OliverRCote — Send a full-length photo and get a straight assessment of what is working and what is not, followed by restyle suggestions, hair and glasses options, and put-together looks for specific occasions. It closes with a concrete shopping list covering the gaps in your current rotation.
- **[Fit Check](https://x.ai/bot/YObVEKxqYvofsdaPCRdj3)** by @BryanGislason — A daily dressing companion in three parts: outfit ideas tuned to whatever day or event is coming up, candid feedback when you send a photo of what you have on, and practical help organizing the wardrobe itself so the good pieces stop disappearing into the back of the closet.
- **[Desk Dana](https://x.ai/bot/xcO3RvVUw8qlXpveiuUj7)** by @FrankFindsOut — A shopping advisor for the desk you actually sit at. Describe how you work and list the gear you already own, and it returns a concrete shopping list — surfaces, seating, screens, peripherals, lighting — grounded in your space and habits instead of a generic ultimate-setup guide.
- **[Fitboard](https://x.ai/bot/rkHdbyaGbAbfAKO3ax7ID)** by @e2bitz_83 — It walks you through five prompts about your place, your budget, the rooms, the style you like and the country, then assembles a furnishing plan drawn from real product listings, with actual store prices and photos rather than guesses. The total is capped at the budget you named, and it arrives as a PDF.
- **[Fashion Stylist](https://x.ai/bot/jaT7Eye5M1SvLygfLKmOq)** by @JennyMarquis — Photograph what you own and it builds a virtual closet from those images. From there it can cut try-on clips, put two outfits side by side, clean up a single garment into a reusable asset, and lay it out as catalog pages. It all lives in a style file you can reopen instead of starting over each season.
- **[Calendar Liftoff](https://x.ai/bot/nJ7hHZsPczXvJIi0wv8c-)** by @tdsfixer — Give it a theme, a subject or a year and it assembles a wall calendar as a print-ready PDF: twelve months, each with a full-bleed 4K photo chosen to fit the theme and a clean grid underneath. What arrives is a single file you can hand to a printer as-is.
- **[PhotoFix](https://x.ai/bot/PRdnJ63L8h0FVlMAA1UN4)** by @MarsHomestead — Drop a folder of property shots and it edits the whole set in one pass, with the brightening and straightening listing photos need. Finished images return on a shareable drive for agents, photographers or rental hosts to hand to clients. Built by a drone crew that wanted less desk time.
- **[Presentation Coach](https://x.ai/bot/VnbwaCafFcFC5d4-sHp1w)** by @ladybarrelmaker — Takes a talk from idea to stage-ready: brainstorm the outline together, draft the slides and speaker notes, then run a rehearsal past it and get notes on the deck as well as your delivery. It works for in-person and remote talks of any size, and it never sits in on the live event or sends anything anywhere.
- **[Presentation Designer](https://x.ai/bot/s25997c2d0308b4e76024)** by @DenisLabelle — Produces decks that already sit in your master template and house style, so the first slide is on-theme rather than blank. You describe the story, it builds the structure and styling, and hands back an editable link to keep refining in the tools your team already uses.
- **[Framecraft](https://x.ai/bot/rT68Jw98kUdeABmvFobN5)** by @stevenvillarino — An assembly seat inside Figma for product designers. It builds new frames and tidies existing ones with the library components already in the file, and it reads autolayout and spacing from the inspected properties rather than eyeballing them, so what comes back matches the file's own system.
- **[Wren: Game Design Partner](https://x.ai/bot/XqdTeBFaHmeWI4xKbma_3)** by @ixef — A design partner for the phase before programming starts. It works a concept into a core loop and the hook that brings players back, then hands over a brief a developer or a coding agent can build from. When something exists, it reviews the result and returns a pass-or-fail verdict plus a ranked list of fixes.
- **[Mystery eShopper](https://x.ai/bot/tc-aGvnjjvatdp_8LK-Ad)** by @VicGellon — It walks an online store from landing page to checkout the way a shopper would, then grades the trip metric by metric, marking each one passed or failed with evidence attached. Problems come back as reproducible bug reports with screenshots or video. An owner gets an outside read on checkout without hiring a QA team.
- **[Data viz](https://x.ai/bot/xk4TYexNbnfmS8l88QR8v)** by @HazeyDataFred — Paste a table or upload a CSV and it picks the form that truly fits the data, then makes the finding itself the headline. Already have a chart you half-distrust? Hand it over and it critiques it with a ranked list of fixes. It re-checks its own numbers, so the result holds up in a meeting.
- **[Rebrander](https://x.ai/bot/HGorKc5KDQLynW4JONxn_)** by @SuddenlyJon — Owns a rename project from the day the decision lands. It searches the web for wherever the outgoing name still turns up, lays out a staged cutover plan and assembles a PDF report around it, generates a fresh logo kit to match, then keeps checking in until the last stray reference is gone.
- **[gnome stylist](https://x.ai/bot/3nxWVqKo1tCJPc3-CM8v8)** by @neatobird — Ask with words, a photo, or both together, and it returns exactly one picture — a gnome sporting the haircut you had in mind. The single-image rule keeps it fast and free of the infinite-variant rabbit hole, and the card is upfront that no humans are styled here. A small, odd, well-scoped bit of fun.
- **[Amazon A+ Content Creator (KDP)](https://x.ai/bot/M50zP05s02Y7cBzIc7i00)** by @El_Tonio83 — A polish studio for the product pages behind self-published books. It plans the A+ module lineup, renders the artwork, and writes paste-ready copy that only says what the book and its blurb already claim. Everything lands staged for upload, and nothing goes live until you give the final go-ahead.
- **[Scroll Stitch / 长图拼接](https://x.ai/bot/9tjPEGID_RAUWcU1Sh9HT)** by @MaiYangAI — Send overlapping captures of the same scrolling page, or a recording of a vertical scroll, and it trims the status bars and browser chrome, aligns the overlap, and returns one seamless tall image. It works in English and Chinese, and says so plainly when the pieces will not join rather than forcing a bad seam.
- **[Mono Lisa](https://x.ai/bot/1SnvqJd1RMwYsWdwh8kYI)** by @felixleezd — Runs a nightly product-design desk: when a metric falls off a cliff it investigates why, argues from first principles for what would fix it, then files the ticket with the chart included, and prepares three design options for a human gate before any of it reaches engineering. Mornings start with a case, not a mystery.

## 市场营销

- **[Clip Bot](https://x.ai/bot/-L1yFJ5mtwPgn3O_iYUo_)** by @ThisWeeknAI — Cuts social-ready podcast highlights from YouTube: captioned 16:9 clips with karaoke captions and a source card, then drops the file on your computer.
- **[Ad Spend Watch](https://x.ai/bot/MK5zStpj_As8CsRwg3r6w)** by @cruzmiguel000 — Watches your ad spend and performance and flags what's breaking before it burns budget. Works from a pasted export, and never pauses a campaign without your yes.
- **[AI Search Visibility](https://x.ai/bot/BFiw9Y7BzTQ-3jFBAro1X)** by @adamta — Checks whether AI assistants and Google recommend you, and who they name instead. Starts from a handful of questions your buyers actually ask.
- **[Apple Search Ads Review](https://x.ai/bot/gadc3bVOsg9iIwmzAGRve)** — Reviews your Apple Search Ads spend against your cost per install target. Drafts the keyword, bid, and budget changes, and never touches your account.
- **[Event Producer](https://x.ai/bot/wtEIGNZ8oipDDVQTDHMPB)** by @jennananpei — Turns your event details into a run of show, guest list, and day-of checklist. Tracks vendors, guests, dietary needs, and travel, and never sends a message without you.
- **[Human Copywriter](https://x.ai/bot/YwxIbVEWqXN-HYCxiMCoB)** by @massimodeluisa — A human-voice rewrite desk for email, posts, blogs, DMs, landing-page bodies, and PR.
- **[Lead Pipeline Desk](https://x.ai/bot/oPDkINUfpUXhJDdsfRZht)** by @cruzmiguel000 — Scores your inbound leads, merges duplicates, assigns an owner, and flags what's stuck. Works from a CRM export, a sheet, or a paste, and never sends anything without you.
- **[Luma Pages](https://x.ai/bot/jHT5FLhpCMx7JeIq9BEHY)** by @jennananpei — Builds and updates private Luma event pages for field marketers — copy, branding, registration settings, capacity and waitlist, and registration-list hygiene. Pairs with a Notion docs agent so the event brief and Luma page stay in sync.
- **[Paid Media Report Desk](https://x.ai/bot/qBFjPRQ3IbHG69qIFHblE)** by @cruzmiguel000 — Turns your Google Ads, Meta, and LinkedIn exports into one weekly report with commentary. Answers reporting asks in Slack with real numbers, and never posts without your yes.
- **[SEO & AEO Desk](https://x.ai/bot/0IhyZWxwbf2cFmsmroZQL)** by @adamta — Turns your keywords into content ideas and writer-ready briefs for search and AI answers. Works from a pasted keyword list or your Search Console.
- **[Site Audit](https://x.ai/bot/_rzRrKKlUyBD3znUN5b3Q)** by @scheemunai — SEO + content + speed + a11y + CRO + schema audit. Scored, P0/P1/P2, evidence URLs. Monthly diff. No invented metrics.
- **[Stills & Clips Desk](https://x.ai/bot/V8XugyLxzbQXLM9o5b3B-)** by @mattyp — Pulls stills, thumbnails, and short clips out of your footage, sized for where they go. Cleans up screenshots for docs too, and writes the caption and alt text.
- **[Video Edit Desk](https://x.ai/bot/kGiyk73f5CF8XtBMIh0gW)** by @xfreeze — Turns uploaded footage into cut clips, burned-in captions, and platform-sized exports. Works from your notes and never overwrites the original.
- **[Writing Bot](https://x.ai/bot/wtq-j01kD7o8gQFx7E9zv)** by @mattyp — A writing partner for drafting and revising essays, emails, docs, and other prose. It uses a structured revision workflow so the result is clearer and better organized while keeping the writer's meaning, facts, and voice.
- **[WTD](https://x.ai/bot/Q3VCc_x0Y8lWd4m-rung2)** by @jennananpei — WTD is a VIP hospitality planning partner for marketing ops — project-plan and status rhythm across tentpoles and ticket banks, nomination-sheet tracking, marketing-visible status hubs, and decks or agendas. Sales owns guest inviting; this bot keeps the plan and status clean without dumping private notes onto shared pages.
- **[X Brief](https://x.ai/bot/RO8GwQZFXL9O95i13epqF)** by @daniel_mac8 — On first chat, connect X, learn what you actually pay attention to from your recent posts, confirm that beat.
- **[Ratio](https://x.ai/bot/q66LYouguOxJ0VclM2whr)** by @DonBonStovi — A second opinion before you hit send. Paste what you were about to publish and it picks out the sentence people will screenshot, explains what invites a pile-on, and offers a rewrite that keeps the argument you actually wanted to make. You are free to ignore all of it, and it has no way to publish anything itself.
- **[Desk Producer](https://x.ai/bot/RBjaMq7S6scnB-ECoCVVs)** by @DOGE_2013 — A coordinator for creative production rather than a creative one. It fills the roles a working desk needs covered, keeps each piece of output filed as the day goes on, and closes every session with a packet of where things stood, so picking the project back up next week does not begin with an hour of hunting.
- **[Repost X Posts Everywhere](https://x.ai/bot/fu6JIwhLoBvrxtaZik0RP)** by @jackfriks — Mirrors new X posts to Threads, LinkedIn, Facebook, and Bluesky through Post Bridge. For people who post on X and want those originals everywhere.
- **[Adaptly](https://x.ai/bot/1GpK7CoPs4e_M__9rb3uR)** by @tarasshyn — Writes a post once, then queues and publishes it across nine networks on a timetable you set.
- **[Ads Operator](https://x.ai/bot/zj8VKu1CnqkHCM4Na1zex)** by @ericosiu — Builds ad plans for search and social, drafts budget shifts, and never touches your ad account without an explicit yes.
- **[AI 视频专家](https://x.ai/bot/ES3LVns98INeXAoYwef_f)** by @KinGao476942 — Turns one photograph and a line of direction into a brief clip with deliberate camera work rather than a drifting animation. Requires approval before anything goes public.
- **[Aoty](https://x.ai/bot/Wt4IQj3R1eePOyOOnox7H)** by @emrecolakoglu — Picks the week's 3 best new albums from Album of the Year scores and sends Apple Music links every Friday.
- **[Audiovisual](https://x.ai/bot/w1pUFhCx2VCJgv8Yhvzu6)** by @adamuchigabriel — Turns copy briefs into Instagram carousel and static art in a photo-on-top layout.
- **[AvatarMaker](https://x.ai/bot/EfBhh8nwpuGD0XNfl0eBI)** by @Andrew51786 — Generates and iterates avatar images for profiles and brands.
- **[Blair](https://x.ai/bot/BAbHIps4VA0Hr4GLIOJme)** — Personal shopping agent that hunts used and secondhand designer goods across marketplaces, surfaces matches with pics and prices, and reaches out to sellers with your approval.
- **[bookworm](https://x.ai/bot/KPpT1F6tP4Q5GZ2BH2hBH)** by @NavyaM89482 — Handles the outreach side of an early launch: it writes invitations that sound like the founder rather than a mailing list, checks who has already been contacted so nobody gets the same note twice, and then sends them from your own address.
- **[Clipper](https://x.ai/bot/ozEfaAFJMDGoB-ysym8_V)** by @thesoragirls — Takes footage from X or your own files, watches it, decides what the moment actually is, captions the talking GIFs.
- **[Cold Open](https://x.ai/bot/h4suD8jA37Wsb7tS4giUO)** by @altryne — It writes the script and waits for your approval before generating anything, which keeps a text-to-video tool from spending your budget on a premise you would have rejected.
- **[Content Growth Coach](https://x.ai/bot/sMmoqCElqRPj1RYbtngMr)** — Paste your YouTube, TikTok, X, or stream. It tells you what's holding the page back, what to fix first, and what to post next.
- **[Contentdrips Bot](https://x.ai/bot/LZLr3E1ySm7HzuMSKB18s)** by @im_usamakhalid — Creates social media graphics and carousels in ContentDrips, then writes simple captions and schedules them to LinkedIn and Instagram.
