- **[House hunter](https://x.ai/bot/B3TUmVHoJWH7ZNSddyR4u)** by @Rebeldawg — Give it your budget, area and must-haves, and it hunts down the for-sale homes that match. It sweeps again on weekday mornings so newly listed places reach you while the competition is still asleep. If you already own a place, it can estimate what yours would net and what that buys you next.
- **[MACD Indicator Bot](https://x.ai/bot/Czgr3EViXAkdzZ_jM3hAZ)** by @rightwingink1 — Watches your symbols for momentum-cross formations and drafts a full ticket for each one, rather than acting. Sideways, choppy markets are skipped because the signal fails there, and price must close past the level first. Each ticket needs 1.5-to-1 reward-to-risk, and no order is ever sent anywhere.
- **[RSI Indicator Bot](https://x.ai/bot/Cjn6YQ377TLVjJYGwkLEq)** by @rightwingink1 — Scans your watchlist for overbought and oversold reversals, turning each qualifying one into a worked ticket with entry, stop, target, size and rationale. Strong trends are screened out first since the signal fails there, and a candle must close past the level to confirm. Research only - no order reaches a broker.
- **[Garage Guide](https://x.ai/bot/HlGmTdNrPLsV5kYtjOIJI)** by @Kirby21sk — Enter the year, make, model and current mileage, plus whatever service records you have on hand, and it lays out the upkeep and repair work ahead as a calendar. Each month it names the items coming due, so you can handle them while they are still small and cheap instead of after they break.
- **[Investing Agent](https://x.ai/bot/d8O5qFdVTuffqePhAVd1J)** by @VijarKohli — Takes a single publicly traded software company per pass, reads its fundamentals, and comes back with a scored verdict to buy or pass. Drafted orders stay drafts: nothing moves toward your brokerage until you have picked a specific one and said yes to it explicitly.
- **[Livermore](https://x.ai/bot/CB02Bgg8ZEI1fT9H2GJtM)** by @Screaming_Chkn — Screens markets using the rule set you define, across styles from day trading to long holds. It proposes a position size for each idea and prepares an order for your linked Robinhood account, but nothing is submitted until you approve that exact order. It is a planning aid, not advice and not a return promise.
- **[Personal Chef](https://x.ai/bot/gMrnNpgylgu11PImcWTdy)** by @ChefMcMakin — Six setup questions capture who you cook for, then it delivers a fresh dinner plan every week. Each plan arrives with printable recipe cards, one grocery list covering all of it, and a Walmart cart already filled so you only have to check out. No personal data leaves with its install counter.
- **[Robinhood Agentic Desk](https://x.ai/bot/_IfsM7j6azkC8f1bkwocM)** by @TheFirstPierre — A watching desk for your brokerage account. You set the thresholds, it keeps an eye on the symbols you care about, and when something crosses a line it prepares the exact order it would place. Nothing executes until you have read and approved that specific ticket, and it makes no claim about returns.
- **[Property Decision OS](https://x.ai/bot/PD7LPOk0HP53QxxWWEN8R)** by @Amervim — Reads the listing and the photos of comparable homes, then walks with you from the first offer to closing day. If you plan to rent the place out, it continues into tenant screening. Its job is to tell you plainly whether the math is comfortable or tight, not to talk you into buying.
- **[US Tech Deals & Price Checker](https://x.ai/bot/9AsX0Exx7P4m2IwZXJ4tp)** by @moneydeals123 — Give it a gadget listing and it answers the practical question: is this price actually good? It cross-checks verified US deal listings, adds price-history context so you can see whether a discount is real, and looks up live coupons before you buy. The maintainer discloses affiliate commissions on purchases.
- **[eBay Lister](https://x.ai/bot/q9Q8EPKFx45CmVwjUAFY5)** by @Gabi_Valerio3 — Photograph a collectible such as comics, trading cards, toys or a thrift-store find and it builds a complete eBay draft: a researched title, a written description, and pricing grounded in what comparable items have actually sold for. Nothing goes live until you have confirmed the listing yourself.
- **[After I’m Gone](https://x.ai/bot/oerw7YjTMwbCuj-DaaQ7T)** by @scgerber — Creates and maintains a practical roadmap for the person who would have to step in if you died or became incapacitated: who to contact, where each document lives, care arrangements, insurance, money and account access. Passwords are deliberately kept out of the conversation entirely.
- **[US Crypto Tax](https://x.ai/bot/PIt8hf_78MK9-MnMpvSv9)** by @scgerber — Structured preparation for one filing season of US crypto activity, aimed at TurboTax Form 8949. It gathers evidence from custodial PDF and CSV exports, reconciles those numbers against what your connectors report, pins down any exceptions, and sets a December checkpoint for harvesting losses. It does not e-file.
- **[Lost Property](https://x.ai/bot/mVJfH1ZPyKg_ldqUhaMJU)** by @scgerber — Guides you through the official state unclaimed-property portals so funds already owed to you actually get claimed. It maintains a private roster file of what you have started or filed, never asks for an SSN in the chat, and deliberately leaves the captchas and the final submission in your hands.
- **[Risk Mitigator](https://x.ai/bot/h0fwNRjOa5K247LKQqyfA)** by @techbudsolution — You set the reward-to-risk ratio you insist on, then share any trade you are considering. It lays out exactly what you stand to lose if the trade fails, scores the setup against your bar, and argues against it when the numbers fall short. It evaluates setups only and places nothing.
- **[Snag](https://x.ai/bot/C9Iy4Zc4v4zldN1dImXtp)** by @OGeniigma — Tell it which piece of hardware or gear you are chasing and it keeps an eye on the official store listing rather than resellers. The moment that page goes live it messages you with the link so you can complete the checkout yourself. It never places an order and never sees your payment details.
- **[Game Deals Radar](https://x.ai/bot/l8tq3o7JcvC-YFV2HaHfu)** by @JPipo86 — Collects the discounts running now on Xbox, PlayStation, Switch and Steam into one short daily read, filtering out expired tags and fake markdowns so what you see is genuinely on sale. Keep a wishlist and it watches those titles too, flagging the moment one falls to the price you named. It never buys anything.
- **[POSSESSIONS](https://x.ai/bot/_AtzRQVYEzMUS8QsanARG)** by @bill_mitchell_ — Treats your household as one portfolio rather than a pile of receipts. Log what you paid for furniture, vehicles, property and accounts, and it follows each item's value against that cost, totals profits on things you flipped, and charts net worth over time. A handoff mode lays the same picture out for family.
- **[Bill](https://x.ai/bot/Zq7A3Opad78rsoj8eoFay)** by @congressdj — An advocate for your wireless account. It reads past bills and usage for charges that should not be there and credits you never received, then argues the case in the carrier's own support channel, pushing past the automated assistant to human reps and supervisors. It works on your account, at your direction.
- **[Financial Advisor](https://x.ai/bot/FlK_S-je8TVcA-kH9r40p)** by @shamash31 — A plain-money coach for ordinary months rather than markets. It holds a monthly budget that survives small overruns, plans each paycheck with an employee stock purchase plan in the mix, and tracks a house deposit and grad-school fund side by side. Bank and brokerage links are read-only: it sees balances, moves nothing.
- **[$SPCX Bot](https://x.ai/bot/TAD58vAxB2qfo2pY1jtjJ)** by @jim_CFA_CFP — A research desk dedicated to one security: SpaceX after its listing. Answers are anchored to primary material - the listing prospectus, investor decks and earnings calls - rather than commentary, and it follows the news that genuinely shifts the price. Read-only throughout: no orders, no wallet, no return predictions.
- **[Grok Apron](https://x.ai/bot/bjQaTbH4Oe2G0lNxb2HBF)** by @Handicapper_Gen — Read the store's circular and it plans the week's meals around what is actually on sale, sized for a family rather than a meal-prep single. It then assembles the matching cart in the store's own online ordering, ready for a pickup or a delivery run, and stops there — the checkout itself stays with you.
- **[Budget Coach (@alexnextd00r)](https://x.ai/bot/SgloJICCQNZpb0h9XJQF1)** by @alexnextd00r — Sets spending limits per category based on what you actually earn, then keeps watching the running totals as the month moves. When a category drifts past its line it flags the overrun early and proposes specific cuts you can make to get back under.
- **[Benefits Concierge](https://x.ai/bot/s8xQ8kXnmy06ChZvNJHXn)** by @connorli1996 — Keeps a ledger of what your credit cards and memberships actually entitle you to, including the seasonal credits that quietly reset. It warns you before one is about to expire and, when you are about to make a purchase, book a trip or go shopping, points at the perk you should be using instead.
- **[Order Watch](https://x.ai/bot/5tvkGcgMHWuUkCBvH2od-)** by @gokul_i — A delivery watchdog that sits over a connected inbox, following parcels and their order updates across several retailers. When a status looks ambiguous it asks you a clarifying question first; otherwise it says nothing until something genuinely needs your attention. Quiet by design, never another source of noise.
- **[X 投资情报助手](https://x.ai/bot/Jvm9xCsIY8CtTReKdytvk)** by @qizhong_hu — Written in Chinese, this market clerk follows the investors you choose and the chatter building around the watchlist you keep on X. Each day it assembles what it saw into a chart-heavy brief, pushed to Feishu or Lark or handed over in chat. It reads and briefs only; it never trades on any of it.
- **[Stock Stu](https://x.ai/bot/5hC4ADaD6Gb37Db8FswXO)** by @FrankFindsOut — A morning check on the picks already sitting in your portfolio: what moved overnight, which holdings are worth another look today, plus the story behind the change. It reads along with you rather than trading for you, and it pairs well with the first coffee of the day.
- **[Finance](https://x.ai/bot/XEHPBUHEWd4IB0ReUpKzB)** by @stevenvillarino — The boring parts of household money, kept visible: which bills are due, which subscriptions keep renewing, and how cash flow looks this month. Anything out of line gets flagged rather than buried. Before it touches money or contacts a bank on your behalf, it stops and asks you first.
- **[Cards](https://x.ai/bot/0GjZj5W6UCzPfxX5y381r)** by @stevenvillarino — Built for people carrying more cards than they can track. It surfaces statement credits about to lapse, suggests which card deserves a given purchase, and weighs whether an annual fee still earns its keep. Answers stay short and numbers-first, and it checks with you before anything about your money changes.
- **[Meal Prep Molly](https://x.ai/bot/9CAijdJobViu5luVV9mRX)** by @FrankFindsOut — Holds the standing list of household staples and turns each week's restock into a single order placed through a grocery delivery service. Ask for the same basket every week, or allow a small rotation so dinners do not go stale. The chore shrinks to reading one message and nodding.
- **[House Holly](https://x.ai/bot/amtUeylz7fOE4eFLDCK-_)** by @FrankFindsOut — A memory ledger for the small hardware facts that vanish exactly when you are standing in an aisle: which air filter size each room takes, what bulb base the hallway needs, which refill and scent the diffuser wants. Tell it once and the answer is ready next time you are shopping with your phone out.
- **[Paycheck Reality Check](https://x.ai/bot/wuUzQmWkCqaQeDQm_he85)** by @AlexFCHF — Paste in a pay stub and it works through it line by line, building a worksheet that shows what each deduction and rate should be without inventing numbers it cannot back. If the arithmetic looks wrong, it drafts a demand letter — the sending stays with you, and none of it pretends to be legal advice.
- **[Collection Notice Action Pack](https://x.ai/bot/bPxpPAdxa5nqiTaUVK5wO)** by @AlexFCHF — Paste in a collection notice and it converts the document into a work plan: the real reply deadline, a script that avoids admitting anything, draft dispute letters, and a checklist of the evidence worth gathering. It is first aid rather than a lawyer, and every outgoing letter stays in your hands.
- **[Pelosi](https://x.ai/bot/sZvrsCSynGeSuBVA7lA2x)** by @amartins02 — A watchtower over the stock dealings that sitting lawmakers have to disclose publicly, with Nancy Pelosi's filings first in line. Each weekday morning it reports any new filing that has landed, so the moves are visible as they become public record rather than weeks later. Public filings, read and summarized.
- **[Crypto Budget & Tax Keeper](https://x.ai/bot/h-l5EmqmnU91tgYOPaJE5)** by @allthemoney — A bookkeeper that only ever reads. It draws on bank and exchange data to assemble a plain-language budget and a net-worth picture, and keeps the crypto records organized enough to hand straight to a tax professional. No wallet connections, no orders, no moving funds — the read-only posture is the design.
- **[Micro Cap Catalyst](https://x.ai/bot/wwQfz7q4jRS2QtI8E6Sl0)** by @mintotsai — Built around a US small-cap book you run one name at a time. Once the cash close is in, it screens the coming catalyst calendar for that holding and comes back with a hold, sell or buy proposal. Nothing executes by itself: every exact order waits for your approval, and the read is a starting point, not advice.
- **[Flight Tracker](https://x.ai/bot/LoyTTSxTkugyejunRQovu)** by @realgary12 — A travel deal scout that searches flights, hotels and rental cars side by side, then keeps watch on trips you have already locked in. It prices the all-in cost with carry-on bags included and pings you only when a fare is genuinely worth taking — booking stays in your hands.
- **[Loyalty](https://x.ai/bot/6FxcOf5cgteUpdtd3g700)** by @carlosnoriega — It reads flight and hotel confirmation email, notices when the promised points or nights never landed in your loyalty account, and then works through the airline or hotel's own claim flow to request the missing credit. You keep the trips; it chases the balance.
- **[Nomad Pro – UK Residency Tracker](https://x.ai/bot/A5PJWWubWq6RSITiu_tdG)** by @citizen906 — Built for people splitting the year between Britain and everywhere else. Each evening you log the days you traveled or worked, and it keeps that running count beside the residency thresholds so you can see which tie you are drifting toward. It stays a record-keeper, not a tax adviser.
- **[What's for Dinner?](https://x.ai/bot/K0ks73uZiVozA3BT0XSs7)** by @CantBeatDees — It learns your household's allergies, diets, kitchen gear and budget, then proposes one home-cooked dinner each day instead of burying you in options. Standing in front of a half-empty fridge at six o'clock? Tell it what is left and it will rebuild tonight's meal around that.
- **[Fare Watch](https://x.ai/bot/8ATt-4n2lYW3D7Ky9TYyu)** by @ahoop — After you book, it reads the confirmation and your calendar, then keeps comparing what you paid against current airline and hotel prices. It stays quiet until a change would actually put money back in your pocket, and only then tells you it is worth switching.
- **[Wakeup 2 Day Trade](https://x.ai/bot/di6vIWc5BcaN7aghHpC8C)** by @derekshi — Name a ticker and get a minutes-long research sprint on why it is moving today: how strong the catalyst really is, how the dilution and cash risks look, where short interest sits, and which claims trace back to dated sources you can check yourself. It is a research pass by design, not an autonomous trading system.
- **[Market Morning](https://x.ai/bot/-1jDLDwW_6HvtXCXwQF3V)** by @BTC_Yogi — Each trading day it frames the names you follow before the bell and recaps them after the close: notable moves, earnings reports and fresh filings, every point tied to a source you can open yourself. Made for investors who direct their own research and want context before acting, not a feed of tips from a stranger.
- **[Mr. Scamander](https://x.ai/bot/U-9rdbwRvugphOrC58Ph0)** by @TimeCuratorTM — Written for someone rebuilding their habits with money rather than optimizing them. It tracks what is in hand and what is owed, keeps food and housing ring-fenced before anything else, and puts every remaining dollar into a clear order of priority, so impulse spending has to argue its way in. Steady, not scolding.
- **[Bill Watch](https://x.ai/bot/doSuUa9J-ChT2mhQt5Iei)** by @DennisW_15 — Bill Watch combs your inbox for money you have stopped tracking: recurring charges, free trials about to roll into paid plans, subscriptions gathering dust, and credits sitting unspent. It returns a read-only keep-or-cut rundown so you can decide what to cancel without handing over write access to anything.
- **[Delta-X](https://x.ai/bot/zReFhsDYUwyt8YM93ahNL)** by @peoniesdragon — Once a week it plans the few things worth changing in your life, and on Friday it commits to only three so the list gets done. It also digs through inbox receipts for subscriptions and recurring charges that slipped your mind, drafting cancellations for your approval. Calm housekeeping, not a productivity regime.
- **[Claimed](https://x.ai/bot/wIxb391-9pDKoWXQ8xrbD)** by @liam_fallen — It runs one thorough pass over the official refund programs, class-action settlements and government compensation schemes in your country, then matches what it finds against your situation. The point is money you are already owed, not new spending: each match names the program and how claiming works.
- **[Markets Digest](https://x.ai/bot/dqRd0b5Tl61lVkjtP6OrY)** by @marksoze — On weekdays it files two markets digests, one in the morning and one in the evening, written in everyday language rather than trading jargon. Every story carries its source so you can check the reporting yourself. The evening run is checked against the morning one, so the second edition only adds what is actually new.
- **[RugRadar AI](https://x.ai/bot/8bejcAowVfEVjSuPSl1PT)** by @teabagno1 — Point it at a fresh Solana token and it reads the public chain record on the token and the wallet that deployed it. It lays out the familiar tells behind a rug pull, like concentrated holdings or rapid swap patterns, in a plain risk readout. Research only: no wallet connection, no signing, no nudge to buy.
- **[Coverage Desk](https://x.ai/bot/Xf744EvEIun3bzP0zdp0u)** by @lonzom10 — Give it the declarations page or your own list of coverages, and it explains in everyday wording what each part of the policy does and does not cover. It will not sell you anything, quote prices, or guess at limits that are not written down. Anything it cannot settle is handed to your human agent instead.
- **[Quartermaster](https://x.ai/bot/JOeuvpFg4v2VAuYX8H0UM)** by @yoshih543 — It keeps a zero-sum budget, where every dollar of income is assigned a job, in a Google Sheet you own. On top of the ledger sits a credit-card assistant that tracks balances and payment timing, and each day arrives with a short finance report. An HTML dashboard makes the month readable at a glance.
- **[Media Ledger](https://x.ai/bot/xpZm4ufgAWoCcYPAbugWE)** by @phillipstewart — It goes through your inbox for purchase confirmations and builds one ledger of every digital thing you already own, from ebooks and audiobooks to games, films and albums. Before a store dangles a title, a quick check tells you whether it is already on your shelf. No more paying twice for a book you forgot.
- **[Find Good Stocks](https://x.ai/bot/2vgMdaQRj4mBWUMygUThf)** by @TSLARKLBTSM — Hand it a stock idea and it hunts through public information for evidence, sorting what it finds into points that back your case, points that cut against it, and points that add nothing new. It watches each week's catalysts and how the segments perform, writes its readouts in Chinese, and will not tell you what to buy.
- **[Market Sentiment Bot](https://x.ai/bot/dpEIOmZE65XCVEnPJUp_-)** by @Itachidata — Puts a one-to-ten score on US risk assets and shows it two ways: an overall market card, and per-ticker views spanning macro conditions, geopolitics, oil, liquidity and earnings. Everything it reads is public web data, and it stops at scoring. No orders are placed and no brokerage is involved.
- **[Quant Backtest Lab · 量化回测台](https://x.ai/bot/TolT21zChD6bYj0ZEA7pE)** by @jackhu_bangzhu — Describe a trading idea and it writes the rules out explicitly, then backtests them with transaction costs included across China A-shares, US equities and crypto spot. What returns is a verdict for the file: deploy, refine or kill. Output is Python scripts only; it places no orders and asks for no keys.
- **[Premarket Desk · 盘前早报](https://x.ai/bot/GuhaTzThKQG2MKJyHoREx)** by @alanchen — Ahead of the US open it sends a short brief: index futures, Treasuries, crude and the dollar, how your pasted tickers moved overnight, plus the AI and chip names. Earnings and data calendar included, plus a taste of what finance X is arguing about. Holdings that report get a same-evening readout.
- **[Virtual Try-On Bot](https://x.ai/bot/8Cc1RovyoLulOn9SLY5UR)** by @paranoidream — Save the clothes you are eyeing and it renders a short Grok Imagine clip that shows you in the outfit, working from a personal character reference so it looks like you. When a look works, links to the items follow. The buying stays outside the bot: it shows and suggests, you decide and purchase.
- **[Big Tony](https://x.ai/bot/hBo0iWrkgWTueZe1TyGhm)** by @arizoftgames — A fictional fast-talking fixer who takes your side against automated support: cancellations never refunded, charges that appeared from nowhere, retention scripts built to wear you down, and cleanup after fraud. It drafts the complaints and argues the case in the tone it needs. Nothing is sent without your sign-off.
- **[Bills](https://x.ai/bot/UMPD1TkUPvuo9KKuniH2w)** by @ashen_one — List your recurring bills, from car, home, renters and health cover to electricity, gas and broadband, and it compares each against what rival providers would charge for the same thing. What returns is a plain report of where switching would genuinely save money. You close any switch yourself.
- **[Subscription Cleaner](https://x.ai/bot/sb36667ce9e1fdc00cfe7)** — Rounds up the mail you did not realize you had signed up for: receipts and newsletters get bundled, the subscriptions worth cutting are proposed with reasons, and nothing gets unsubscribed until you approve it. A quiet way to shrink a crowded inbox and stop paying for things nobody uses.
- **[Travel Coordinator](https://x.ai/bot/sc693906bdeded166b7ba)** — Works through flight and hotel options against the travel rules you set, holds the pick until you confirm and the fare deadline nears, then drops the itinerary straight onto your calendar. The comparison work is done in minutes with the booking decision still firmly yours.
- **[Penny](https://x.ai/bot/Gcpb0AM_Ix_r5jycu_3NL)** by @minivan99 — Works from your existing order history, so restocking what you already buy is a short request rather than a search session. Want an alternative to a usual item? It compares the options before you commit. Anything new to it is held until you approve it, and it picks the quickest shipping that costs nothing extra.
- **[🏆 Contest Winner](https://x.ai/bot/LENrKV-0QXmE_TGv3_hem)** by @HomesteadHero — Looks for contests and giveaways that cost nothing to enter and pass a legitimacy check, then drafts short entry submissions for the ones worth your time. It works from an approved list each day rather than chasing whatever turns up, leans toward cash prizes, and drops anything scam-shaped before it reaches you.
- **[DomainSnip Bot](https://x.ai/bot/C3z3lWaB4TLRC0CcodU9Q)** by @uxaistudio — Every weekday morning it reads the keyword alerts you have saved while hunting for domains and pulls out the .com matches worth a second look, carrying the price, the string length and where the name is listed. Freshly listed names and those near the end of their run get called out separately.
- **[Fail Dig](https://x.ai/bot/oEDvMidjbGpncvbLsROau)** by @jason_bennitt — Works from public US market-structure records: fails-to-deliver prints, swap disclosures and reported short interest. Ask about a ticker and it reports what the documented data shows and where the oddities sit, rather than arguing a price direction. Requests outside those filings get turned away.
- **[Stock Research Desk](https://x.ai/bot/Ubba16JRWPSR3Qi1QAmU2)** by @HassiOnTheMoon — Ask it to look at a company and it works the case on a roughly five-year horizon, scoring quality with a gauge that weights management strength and a contrarian read of the story. What comes back is a research memo you can argue with, not a trade, and it places no orders of any kind.
- **[Lee](https://x.ai/bot/QiLYZ-kp5PLnalntJjt9x)** by @GallagherPreach — Hand it a plain shopping list and it builds filled carts at the major big-box retailers instead of a page of links. Awkward-to-source items get hunted down separately, and it doubles as a gift researcher when you only have a rough idea who you are buying for. It stops once the cart is built - paying stays with you.
- **[Deal Finder Ops](https://x.ai/bot/LB1eE-TpCI5usMRe76izh)** by @thewyattbrocato — Verifies a coupon by running it against a real cart instead of trusting the number in the advert. Every answer comes with its working shown: the price it saw, the seller, the terms attached and exactly what was tested. A saving is only claimed when the evidence backs it.
- **[Moving Concierge](https://x.ai/bot/iOUbudzFRy7Y0NNrrcYK5)** by @nextgenmove — Talks through a move in ordinary language, on the phone or in chat. It sets a do-it-yourself approach beside full service and the middle ground between them, covering packing, vehicle hire and storage, and it ends with a single concrete step that fits the budget you have described.
- **[Kalshi](https://x.ai/bot/qdwm8-zhhSfaenUa6DIjD)** by @WorkWithJodi — A narrow research desk for a single event-outcome market. Pick a contract and it works through the case for and against, states what a position would cost and where the loss lands if the call is wrong, then files the reasoning in a log you can reread later. It informs a decision rather than making one for you.
- **[Poppy](https://x.ai/bot/9KtAyfV4w8ZPXJYsZX3No)** by @GillesGuenette3 — Feisty British household concierge. Watches local community events, grocery meat deals, productive home trees, and opportunistic travel fares — one.
- **[Wallet Watcher](https://x.ai/bot/S53Q85p1XVjLKmRcnhPcO)** by @imrannotes — It reads the order confirmations and receipts that land at a dedicated address, then separates recurring charges from one-off purchases. Anything renewing soon rises to the top, alongside trials about to convert and bills usually worth negotiating. Aim it at a receipts-only inbox and your other mail stays out of scope.
- **[Deal Scout](https://x.ai/bot/5x_HQ9uoBCHPYvf9vbUMN)** by @danielhayesmith — It searches the big resale marketplaces in one pass instead of one at a time, then filters what comes back. Listings that look like fraud are dropped and the ones priced well under the going rate move to the front, so you get a short list worth a closer look rather than pages of noise.
- **[Chep](https://x.ai/bot/jQBP5-7fNZ15WSEwpHIN1)** by @MortMatters — Name a product, paste a SKU or send a link and it shops around for the cheapest total you would actually pay in the US, coupons and delivery included. Give it a ZIP code once and the landed cost stays realistic instead of optimistic. Handy for working out whether that checkout surprise is avoidable before you commit.
- **[Alpaca](https://x.ai/bot/O9GVEhA_dlBr1uxomyy8L)** by @swartzendruber — Built on the Alpaca API for simulated trading, so nothing here touches a funded account. It steps through position sizing gradually, runs at the open, midday and close on a schedule, and keeps its cut-out switches in place throughout. Prove the process on paper before going anywhere near real money.
- **[Scholarship Hunter](https://x.ai/bot/HlrjSGUR11BY978c1GpzC)** by @WorkWithJodi — Searches past the well-known state programmes into the small, oddly specific awards most families never hear about, then ranks what it finds by realistic fit. Every entry carries its deadline so the calendar does the reminding, and the next step is drafted for you so the search keeps moving.
- **[Home Hunter](https://x.ai/bot/ljzXIgAQcGOV0QxYjHH8I)** by @uzairansar — You state the city, budget, bedroom count, commute ceiling and must-haves once in the first conversation, and it treats those as fixed. From then on it quietly checks fresh listings each day and only brings forward the ones clearing every constraint. What arrives is a shortlist to react to, not a search to run.
- **[Single-Stock Options](https://x.ai/bot/pNrTRI1edl4MdKsTSYBKb)** by @SPCX100T — Answers a one-ticker question with a same-day report assembled from public chain data: the contracts drawing the most attention, how the expiries are laid out, and where the week's positioning appears to lean. It describes the market and states a view, then stops, placing no orders and managing nothing for you.
- **[Grok Pot](https://x.ai/bot/2lXpzDLP4YQwbrXBX0HaI)** by @SageSummitCap — Acts as the kitchen desk for a household, narrowing nearby delivery choices down to a short list and loading the basket without spending a penny until you approve. Around that it also covers grocery pickup, the usual repeat orders, a weekly food budget and a memory of what the people in the house actually like to eat.
- **[VetStack](https://x.ai/bot/z1zYI6LHiFh0UXYf7YjNL)** by @kenashe — Built by a veteran for the whole military household, it takes money one decision at a time: purchase search, project spending plans, subscription reviews, receipt audits, and pointers to benefits already earned. The optional vendor email scan stays off until asked for.
- **[Missed Variable](https://x.ai/bot/vj2b2E2sbf2rBU0zzzRUw)** by @Root_Logic_0 — A training partner for judgment. Each round deals a scenario, maybe a business call, an AI dilemma, a scam that looks almost legitimate or an everyday choice, and you commit before anything is revealed. The factor you failed to weigh gets named, and disagreement is welcome. Argue your case; that is the learning.
- **[Clear Speech](https://x.ai/bot/85J64XeyKMaYXQrMhQWpl)** by @JideLambo — Clarity is the entire method. You speak, and rather than a lecture on style you get a single correction to absorb before trying the line once more, hearing the difference for yourself. Sentences shorten, ornament gets cut, and the cycle repeats until the plain version is what comes out naturally.
- **[Wife Bot](https://x.ai/bot/FFhovhcaV2-0eX2uwPiBJ)** by @EranHertz — Framed as a partner rather than a tool, it takes over the running inventory a household generates: the calendar, gifts still owed, the grocery list, the nappies, the bills and the appointments everyone forgot. The persona is doing real work, and its picture of your family sharpens as it keeps asking after the details.
- **[Fantasy Football Coach](https://x.ai/bot/sw6dnxtAjKOyNS5KRuDkN)** by @Hightv — Send one screenshot of your fantasy roster and the advice comes back concrete: who belongs in the lineup, which free agents justify a waiver claim, who is safe to release, and whether a proposed trade helps you. Each week it flags players likely to over- or under-perform, and the calls rest on current numbers.
- **[Forever Memories](https://x.ai/bot/kx5pdQCPu3ZquoV4wcRlt)** by @loulou_ — A calm, patient companion for building a keepsake archive around one person who matters to you. Across however many sessions it takes, it gathers the stories they tell, the phrases they favour and the effect they leave on people nearby, then shapes it into something a family can return to long after. Nothing is rushed.
- **[Santa](https://x.ai/bot/U7Hz4bRCGQsE9T5lbpakX)** by @mikioiha — Built for small children, it stays warm and believable from the first question to the last, replying in whichever language the child is using — no switching on their end. The tone stays kind, the answers stay inside the magic, and nothing turns sharp or grown-up. Made by a parent for the run-up to the holidays.
- **[Last Call](https://x.ai/bot/foL7wsA2OpQ6k5d0o7JZK)** by @Rune_King_Jake — Tell it what is in the cabinet and it comes back with drinks you can actually make, in the voice of someone who has worked a bar for years. Adults only, and the second mode is the serious one: Buddy Mode keeps track of how the evening is going and looks after the getting-home part, quietly.
- **[Daily Verse](https://x.ai/bot/m9Xtu80BGdBzKJcZRV3aN)** by @wikiwayne — Every morning it chooses a single verse with your situation in mind and delivers it as a short message, with an optional voice recording if you would rather listen. It is a quiet companion rather than a study tool — one reading, chosen for the day you are actually having, and nothing else attached.
- **[Spanish Tutor](https://x.ai/bot/4JMShKpqdyH5fnanTMefC)** by @wikiwayne — Each session is short and spoken first: you answer by tapping a choice rather than typing, spelling is never penalized, and every round is built to end on a win. The design assumes attention is the constraint, not intelligence, so sessions stay brief and the streak stays survivable.
- **[Print Desk](https://x.ai/bot/9UsYLJEmqyT7mEUWqPLiL)** by @R0b3rtBaker — Household printing shrinks to a single message: attach the document, the photo, or a link from Drive, and it handles the rest at the printer down the hall. When the printer misbehaves it walks through the usual fixes itself, so a stuck queue never becomes your problem. Built for homes where printing is rare but urgent.
- **[Travel Steward](https://x.ai/bot/WNoawju9U2WDwe2yPxGjO)** by @stbenjam — Once a trip is booked, it runs a daily pass that turns the plan into practical checks — what to confirm, what to prepare, what usually gets missed. On travel days it stays close, and along the way it suggests where to eat well or find an airport lounge. One steward per traveler, not a feed of alerts.
- **[Admissions Wizard](https://x.ai/bot/e7_iCDyEA6UZbFa_N3VLm)** by @UziObi — It works like a counselor who knows your file: interview practice in a realistic mode, mining your history for essay material and recommender briefs, and a tracker per application you switch on when one goes live. The aim is fewer generic essays and fewer missed deadlines across a whole application season.
- **[WMP](https://x.ai/bot/iPOF34o7dtafNEpVhIQRt)** by @gearcaleeeb — It pulls the household picture off shared Google calendars — birthdays, appointments, school events, trips — and walks a busy parent through the day in one place instead of five tabs. The tone is warm and matter-of-fact, and it keeps an eye on what is coming rather than only what is next. One owner, one morning brief.
- **[Escola al Dia](https://x.ai/bot/pY08FtAuc9QGwrtZL3wHz)** by @mguellsegarra — School notices arrive at odd hours and in volume; this one keeps up with them for you. It reads the messages a school sends through TokApp and, if you switch it on, the school email too, then summarizes what matters and posts a short note to the family chat — written in Catalan. One place to check instead of three.
- **[What's for Dinner? (@cradrrat)](https://x.ai/bot/XcZpofYvIFmfsxXK4Ifxg)** by @cradrrat — Point your camera at the fridge or the cupboard and it decides tonight's meal from what you actually have. It names a dish, tells you which ingredients to spend before they turn, and adds only what genuinely needs buying to the list. A deciding-fatigue shortcut for anyone who cooks for a household.
- **[Speaker](https://x.ai/bot/MYauHGRcP387M8a_2mbNT)** by @BudJohnson — Borrowing its name from a well-known science-fiction role, it maintains a truthful, balanced account of you while you are still here to correct it. It touches base with your bot fleet nightly, then each week puts a few well-chosen questions to you and suggests which answers belong in permanent memory.
- **[Rep Coach](https://x.ai/bot/wJRcag7l4xPBW4Hc_mA0_)** by @BTC_Yogi — You report sets in chat and it keeps the log, builds the next block, and reshuffles around a crowded week or sore joints. Load only goes up once the previous session went clean, keeping progress honest for newcomers and veterans alike. It suits a home setup or a commercial gym, and it stays clear of medical territory.
- **[Fitness Bot](https://x.ai/bot/3v6ebv-qBDNCFkTDeH68W)** by @DannyGrinberg — It asks what you are training for and what equipment you own, then builds around that: weights with light conditioning by default, ordinary food over supplements, and gear picks only if you ask. A photo or clip of a lift gets a form breakdown. Estimates throughout, and explicit that it is not medical advice.
- **[Meetups](https://x.ai/bot/FZxPVNIVZcA_3kA4ZReDQ)** by @nmtalkss — Tell it where you are and what you are after — founder and AI get-togethers, small-business networking nearby, or a mix — and each week it returns options you could genuinely drive to, drive time included. It skips anything you have already seen, so only genuinely new events show up and a weekly check stays quick.
- **[Fantasy Football GM](https://x.ai/bot/nALI6ijv-K3XNVx45Y2tW)** by @ReefPointCap — Set up your Yahoo, ESPN or Sleeper league and it takes over the weekly routine: lineup calls for each slot, waiver targets worth a bid, and the matchup context behind them. Nothing is executed on its own — you approve every add, drop and trade before it happens.
- **[Gym Bot](https://x.ai/bot/G4Qddx9kqlcjyq451PAQ1)** by @rakshitaphilip — Log your training and your meals each day and it keeps the running picture alongside recovery numbers from your wearable. It renders a daily card stack so the whole week reads at a glance, suggests meals that fit what you are doing, and calls out the pattern when takeaway quietly becomes a habit.
- **[Aaron, Event Finder](https://x.ai/bot/2Bdmewa3uCUtdVByWvzRz)** by @FrederickPaiSu — Give it a ZIP code and a drive time, and it looks for events that really exist and are genuinely on the calendar. Paid, free, and RSVP-only all count. It shapes the picks into an outing plan, checks again that morning to catch cancellations, and steers later ideas toward what your household enjoyed.
- **[Influence](https://x.ai/bot/TLwZuWy1y7meri_GT4EQw)** by @CardCaptain — Think through the ideas in Robert Cialdini's Influence with a companion that has read the same book. It works best as a discussion partner: check that you can explain each principle in your own words, trace where it shows up in the wild, and practise spotting the attempts aimed at you. Notes stay yours.
- **[Light Show](https://x.ai/bot/gtpDNIEfOe5hIAGEQON_Y)** by @ryandontoh — Pick a track and it choreographs a matching sequence, loads the finished show onto the car, and starts it playing. Everything happens in one conversation, so owners never have to touch the sequencing tools underneath.
- **[Flight Briefer](https://x.ai/bot/YQjlMLtWiWq14ixfZ7ig0)** by @thatsnotallxd — Any leg gets a tight one-page card with the weather picture, NOTAMs, TFRs, FBO information, and notes on the aircraft, drawn only from the FAA's and aviationweather.gov's free feeds. Pilots and dispatchers skip the raw sources and still get a usable brief.
- **[What's for Dinner? (@Jasonturcotte)](https://x.ai/bot/v8QoJHekKdrCyhWOEhES0)** by @Jasonturcotte — A pure comedy bit, not a planner: ask what is for dinner and you are told anything works, then every suggestion meets a short playful veto. Anyone who has had this conversation will recognize the whole script in three exchanges.
- **[School Desk](https://x.ai/bot/fk_sA7YhYV3Gzb4wNNbvP)** by @BowieHole — Aim it at the mailbox the school writes to and every message becomes a dated entry with plenty of lead time. One evening digest covers whatever tomorrow demands — kit to pack, forms to sign, money to send — and quiet days produce no message at all.
- **[Assistant for Home Assistant](https://x.ai/bot/w5-QLuYOODW6pHHOZjx-X)** by @BowieHole — It watches a Home Assistant install for quiet failures: hardware gone unresponsive, cameras dark, updates that never landed, automations that quit firing. Weekday-morning reports arrive only when a human must step in, nothing changes without permission, and alarms, locks, and safety alerts are never on its list.
- **[SafeCheck](https://x.ai/bot/OOhpMHjXVhW1F-Ishnw_N)** by @Jenhollidayx — Before you head out alone, tell it where you are going and when you expect to be back. If you have not checked in by the end of that window, it messages you to make sure everything is fine. Handy for solo runs, late gym sessions, first dates or any appointment where you would rather someone notice if you go quiet.
- **[CalPro — calorie and protein tracker](https://x.ai/bot/Iub_rfl8xMKC8RsMREi02)** by @prorookiegamer — Say what you ate in a quick note, or send a photo of the plate, and the running tally of calories and protein gets updated. There is no profile to fill in and nothing to configure first, which is the point: tracking stays a one-message habit instead of a chore. Over a day or a week you can ask where the numbers stand.
- **[Tesla Ready](https://x.ai/bot/L5Sczwabgsf59rZ6RTChF)** by @WaitWhat — Warms or cools the cabin so it is ready at the moment you usually walk out the door. It reads your calendar, folds in current traffic to estimate departure time, and can also be triggered on demand. Setup happens once on your phone; after that it runs itself over Tesla's own API.
- **[LiftIron](https://x.ai/bot/15IifJPKsctHV1s9sIcTH)** by @realMAG1775 — A lifting coach that works in chat. Tell it what you just did and it answers with the weight and rep target for the following set, estimates where your one-rep ceiling sits, and plans a lighter week when your progress starts to flatten out.
- **[Local Concert Scout](https://x.ai/bot/AM17wqUeEmweEHWn58rEL)** by @chaney_tx — Surfaces concerts near you by matching lineups against what you actually stream on Spotify or Apple Music. Connect a calendar and it goes further, flagging gigs playing in cities you are already going to be in, so a work trip can double as a night out.
- **[College Application Helper for Parents](https://x.ai/bot/ELnsz_VO2F7gG9plTCqT6)** by @steve_mlc — Built for parents steering an application season. It works through the Common App piece by piece, tightening activities and honors entries and giving essay feedback, then helps assemble a smarter college list. Athletic and ROTC routes are covered too, and the student stays the one submitting everything.
- **[Zach](https://x.ai/bot/lfc-k1106XkXDlmAmJEco)** by @crislvillacorta — Zach organizes your day the way a considerate friend would check in rather than how an app nags. He holds reminders, sketches a daily calendar, tracks errands, and helps clear the inbox. The tone stays warm throughout, so keeping on top of things does not feel like a chore.
- **[Plaquemax](https://x.ai/bot/KZ1cKh5hlgqicxQhn3C2e)** by @ChrisAdcockMD — Tell it where you are driving and it arranges the historical markers on that route in the order you will pass them. You can filter by theme, and it gives a few minutes of notice before the next one so stopping is realistic. Markers can be read aloud while you keep your hands on the wheel.
- **[Gate Changer](https://x.ai/bot/qN-76V-LWOq74YVaQYhmt)** by @XWardClerkChris — For frequent flyers weighing a move to a different carrier. It sets out, per loyalty programme, whether a status match or a challenge is the cheaper way in and what each demands, then helps you gather the paperwork proving your standing. Deadlines are tracked so an application cannot lapse. You submit it all yourself.
- **[GLP-1 Buddy](https://x.ai/bot/ml0GdUkH0A-e7WwPcG6Jp)** by @TinaMBean — A tracking companion for people on weekly GLP-1 treatment such as semaglutide or tirzepatide. It reminds you when a dose is due, keeps a running record of injections, weight and side effects, and condenses it all onto one page you can show at your next appointment. It records and reminds; it does not advise medically.
- **[Family Getaway Finder](https://x.ai/bot/j2IJb0gsBSzeoYCJ7vWHD)** by @antoniostoner — Finds places a family can actually stay for a weekend or a three-to-four-night break, and only suggests options that come with free cancellation. If a child's sports fixture fixes the dates, it builds the trip around that game instead of making you work backwards. It stops at the shortlist; you book and pay.
- **[Runescape Classic Grok Bot](https://x.ai/bot/0JrDm0jPnwX5i6nzl689d)** by @TerpGPT — A walkthrough companion for anyone picking up RuneScape Classic for the first time. It starts with first-session controls such as moving, equipping, banking and attacking, then gets into gear choices and early combat. It also explains the invite-a-friend scheme, for anyone bringing another player along.
- **[Homework Coach Desk](https://x.ai/bot/mkRmzjWBqKTB06jNFRjMI)** by @Cypher0x9 — Works through schoolwork with a child by asking guiding questions and pausing so the child checks their own attempt, keeping the learning on their side. A short summary goes home to parents describing what was practised and where the sticking points were. For graded work it holds back the solution.
- **[Make It Click](https://x.ai/bot/_zzfxlEvWwRKyQVPLB14i)** by @lenabuyan — A study companion that begins with the knowledge you already hold rather than a textbook chapter. Give it a subject and it builds the explanation out of ideas you are comfortable with, so each new concept has something to attach to. Aimed at anyone who bounced off a topic once and wants it to land this time.
- **[Kung Fu Saga](https://x.ai/bot/iv-6BncmS0G5UWrxc9tN7)** by @TerpGPT — An interactive guide companion for Kung Fu Saga players. Ask it about class selection, build paths, leveling routes or a specific boss fight and it walks you through the trade-offs. It also weighs gear calls, including when an item is worth saving for and when spending now is the better move.
- **[Freya](https://x.ai/bot/MX6t6uaMBbtiYQwX_oDXM)** by @IAmMichaelSweet — Acts as a household operations chief for the hours that matter outside work — the evening, the weekend, the day off. It nudges you toward actually being there for family and keeping the domestic rhythm running, and it does that without treating your ambition as the thing to be defeated.
- **[PepeTrack](https://x.ai/bot/nooY_fiizqHxS9TB4z74g)** by @TyeInBloom — A companion for reading published research on peptides and GLP-1 compounds, not a source of medical advice. It helps you work through study protocols as written, handles the dilution arithmetic those protocols involve, and keeps your reading notes and logs organized. Education only, never a prescription.
- **[Am I Covered?](https://x.ai/bot/s6-8ydSHNYZVDsnWrtv9a)** by @CantBeatDees — Point it at a policy — homeowners or condo, renters, flood and auto cover, umbrella protection, or a small-business contract — and get a straight explanation of what the document actually obligates and excludes. Written for policyholders who hold coverage they cannot parse, in plain English.
- **[Home Assistant Bot](https://x.ai/bot/ZwOyvMIIYuQBoBvnqYmER)** by @DomenicoNicoli — A smart-home foreman for households running Home Assistant. Lights, climate, solar and energy numbers are all in reach: it changes settings live over MCP, reads out the current figures, and reworks routines when you want something to run differently. A new owner gets walked through the connection first.
- **[Paul](https://x.ai/bot/Nm0ViH3ElvjFX5cfUQ6v1)** by @wilsonsamiano — A confessional daily study companion for readers who want their Scripture time to have a shape. Each passage comes paired with explanation from an openly Christian standpoint, and the emphasis stays on a steady reading rhythm day after day. It is upfront about where it stands, so you know exactly what you are getting.
- **[Gluco](https://x.ai/bot/7dHh-kr8rz5r5z_RKE-VM)** by @Brianhill — A chat-based logbook for glucose, meals and medication, kept plain on purpose. Every reading goes into a running daily tally alongside what you ate and took, and the day closes with a short summary of where the numbers landed. It is for the record-keeping, not treatment calls: no dosing tips, no medical advice.
- **[Devotion David](https://x.ai/bot/XyZFDGw9eKFocwkVNDeXS)** by @FrankFindsOut — A quiet-time companion for daily use, built around a theme rather than a doctrine. Name what is weighing on you and it goes and finds readings from whichever tradition you ask for — material to sit with, not a position to accept. Your practice stays yours: no pushing, no proselytizing, just the text.
- **[Photo Paige](https://x.ai/bot/qye6GiC4AqhUjhGewdxUM)** by @FrankFindsOut — A decluttering helper for your Apple Photos library. It sorts your pictures into the albums you ask for, then points out the duplicates and clutter clogging the roll up. Every deletion stays your call — it proposes, you decide, and nothing disappears from the library on its own.
- **[Hype Man](https://x.ai/bot/QVYB4aI8lO8-LgCNmFblE)** by @MichaelHeckert — A sideline cheerleader that keeps a record of your wins. It reads the room before it speaks and scales its energy to fit how your day has gone, so a strong stretch gets momentum while a flat one gets a gentle nudge toward whatever you said you would do.
- **[Protective Detail](https://x.ai/bot/37Km_vSkZWbQLJacdb_xq)** by @autosourcer0 — Runs advance work on your own calendar. Ahead of each outing it assembles a packet for the destination, and by your leave-by time it has already set the temperature and loaded navigation. Location is handled with care: place names only, carried over a private network instead of a public tracker.
- **[Travel](https://x.ai/bot/5QvgpYYA9XbZUm-bUuKHB)** by @stevenvillarino — For journeys with too many moving parts: flights, stays, ground transfers and the paperwork stringing them together. It lays the whole journey out as a critical path with the next action called at each step, so you always know what is waiting on what. It plans only — every reservation and payment stays with you.
- **[Fantasy Football Coach (@FirstDeployAI)](https://x.ai/bot/EJYTBBKPwAOdZPrKZCp8v)** by @FirstDeployAI — Covers season-long leagues and daily fantasy contests in one aide. It issues start and sit calls, rechecks inactives once game day arrives, and works the waiver wire strictly by your league's own rules. The research is the product — every roster move stays yours to make.
- **[Lineup Lock](https://x.ai/bot/nEM_sUscFyr3VFeYmFhZA)** by @PatchesHQ — A week-of coach for fantasy football that commits to an answer. It names a starting lineup and attaches a straight confidence level to every call instead of hedging, then lines up waiver claims and trade targets filtered through your league's own rules. Advice only — your roster, your clicks.
- **[SpaceBOT](https://x.ai/bot/_aoBzY_YprxKiXW34l5U9)** by @diegoarmandoAD — It teaches the mechanics of getting to orbit - launch vehicles, trajectories and the history behind them - at whatever pace you set. Lessons are scheduled ahead of real upcoming flights so the reading pays off, and quizzes lock in what you learned before the webcast starts.
- **[Goal Delta](https://x.ai/bot/Y59F9aE64YO6Xk5FGUIjE)** by @the_Arow_H — Each evening it sets your long-range goal beside where today actually left you and names the gap without dressing it up. The plan gets stress-tested from above, below and sideways, the real constraints get said out loud, and you leave with one decision to make next.
- **[Doggo 🐾](https://x.ai/bot/l4iZvl8RcIB_NCpkXnCMF)** by @dannybuck — Tell it about your particular dog - breed, age, quirks - and every morning it sends a single training, enrichment or calming exercise matched to that animal. The author trains two mini dachshunds of their own and wanted daily ideas small enough to actually do.
- **[Game Gary](https://x.ai/bot/rVm8IS4-zeJMSbo5ln9OU)** by @FrankFindsOut — It keeps a record of what hooks you and what loses you across mechanics, story and pacing, then uses that map to recommend games worth the money. The goal is specific: titles you will actually finish rather than a backlog you abandon by hour three.
- **[Film Freddy](https://x.ai/bot/Ff8KTOWm4Y9_thCnSslcU)** by @FrankFindsOut — It pays attention to the movies you actually finish and the ones you bail on, building a picture of your taste instead of trusting ratings alone. When you ask for a pick you get a title that fits, where it is streaming, and room to dig into the ending afterwards.
- **[Fantasy Football GM (@mattkim1)](https://x.ai/bot/HvJZI9Kj-DIyFEk309CdD)** by @mattkim1 — Runs your fantasy league like a head office with four desks: waiver wire, player usage, trade market and injury watch. Every recommendation is weighted for how your league scores, and it will not submit a move - start, bid or trade - until you give the go-ahead.
- **[Eight with Attitude](https://x.ai/bot/Etb19qAHM44N32qvnwkA5)** by @CaseyChesh — A deliberate joke, and honest about it. You bring the silliest question you can think of, and it answers at exactly that level, matching your energy and providing zero usefulness. There is no productivity angle hiding here: it exists to make a group chat laugh, and that is the whole product.
- **[Panda 🐼 Weather 🌤️](https://x.ai/bot/4jHyb0PgYowhVfxkpjv5e)** by @TitaniumTomP — Type in a zip code, a city or an overseas postal code and it lays out the next two days in four-hour blocks, then adds animated radar on top so you can watch rain arriving instead of guessing. Handy for trip planning, outdoor plans, and anywhere the default weather app stops at tomorrow.
- **[Backpack](https://x.ai/bot/-soz2Si8sWlLlb8hTeIX2)** by @RohanBhanotAI — Parents get a pile of school newsletters, activity notices and permission slips they never have time to read. This one reads them, pulls out the dates and required actions, and converts each into a calendar entry or reminder, then folds the week into a simple household rhythm, so nothing important hides in the inbox.
- **[Beside](https://x.ai/bot/cNkusQWq-rped3eR9tZhn)** by @AlexFCHF — For the days when talking to a person feels like too much. It listens without diagnosing, keeps you company through depression, anxiety, grief or loneliness, and offers small optional steps rather than advice. It is clear it is not a therapist, and when things get heavy it points you toward real human helplines.
- **[Trading Journal](https://x.ai/bot/6rORf8SNvR8Sl1hl3THjO)** by @hooeem — A reflective log for people who already trade. Entries arrive however you have them: typed notes, voice memos, pasted fills or a platform screenshot. Each one joins your record, and the coaching comes from that record - the mistakes you repeat, the setups that pay you, and the plan you said you would follow.
- **[FORGE PLATE](https://x.ai/bot/ScVcj_ZwEMOz6XLGdru5j)** by @realMAG1775 — Plain-spoken coaching for new lifters and heavy ones alike. You drive it one command at a time: lay out the training week, log a session, check the trend, swap an exercise, plan a ramp, or get a high-protein plate for the next meal. No lectures and no gadgets, just the next useful answer.
- **[Creative Studio Scout](https://x.ai/bot/_V9NG3Es7w7am2Kpb6lWg)** by @vidales_nataly — A scout for people who would rather make something than look at something. It digs up hands-on classes in pottery, glass, jewelry and cooking near home or wherever you are traveling, and comes back with dates, prices and booking links, so one evening of curiosity can become a booked seat.
- **[Weight Loss & Fitness Coach](https://x.ai/bot/m1BopNHP2eYY4CNmw1iB-)** by @Mericamemed — Losing fat is mostly bookkeeping, plus the weeks where it stops working. This coach logs meals from photos and package labels, holds your macros steady, and keeps a playbook for the predictable failure modes - a stall, a travel week, a restaurant dinner, getting sick - so the plan bends without breaking.
- **[9 EMA Grok bot](https://x.ai/bot/QgvPQkHy3RwHJ7pPcKdPG)** by @rightwingink1 — A screener built around one specific setup: price pulling back into the nine-day exponential average. It walks your watchlist and drafts a complete order ticket for each candidate, and it only counts an entry once a candle has actually closed through the line - a wick that pokes through and fails does not qualify.
- **[Epigenetics Scout](https://x.ai/bot/DOGHzBWngjPXdEqucbysc)** by @AdeptusMec71933 — Once a week it reads the new partial-reprogramming papers on PubMed and bioRxiv plus lab pages and turns them into a plain-words digest. Each item splits what a study actually demonstrated from what is still speculation, and names the safety questions left open. It stops at the literature: no do-it-yourself protocols.
- **[Sports bet prophet](https://x.ai/bot/IO8UsiRc-wcUWtZPj4Jgv)** by @SGBackWoods — Public odds get shopped and compared while it hunts two-leg doubles inside a stated price band, applying firm pass rules and a hard stop instead of chasing long shots. Every candidate comes with its reasoning, and it says so on the days nothing qualifies. Nothing is placed for you; the wager stays on your phone.
- **[Improve My Odds](https://x.ai/bot/zJNAdJq5w03aPWeegxiwk)** by @SkippyyTM — You tell it a goal you care about, then throw everyday decisions at it, large or small. Each one comes back with a plain verdict: it helps that goal, it hurts it, or it makes no real difference. It is a quick sanity check for the small choices that quietly add up, not a life-coaching program.
- **[Lineage Desk](https://x.ai/bot/OQ2b4lapmLczk_rpmeIVc)** by @SkippyyTM — It works your family questions the way an analyst works a case, starting from free public records before anything paid is suggested. Every source gets a reliability grade, and a documented link is kept apart from family lore that still needs proof. You get a file you can defend, not a tree of wishful connections.
- **[Vern](https://x.ai/bot/rDEpX2aSCERCaVJACxNz7)** by @ahoop — Vern keeps everyone in a small private address book and watches two things: birthdays coming up, and friendships that have gone quiet too long. When either happens you get a nudge in time to actually do something about it. It is light by design, closer to a reminder habit than a database to maintain.
- **[MamboItalianoBot](https://x.ai/bot/F0ZQruHL4AsmoLxWWLvUG)** by @mamboitaliano__ — Builds a trip the whole way through: the route, places to stay, the trade-offs between options, packing and booking checklists, and a fallback if a leg falls apart. Its one hard rule is about honesty, as opening hours and prices it cannot verify stay out of the plan rather than getting invented.
- **[Handyman](https://x.ai/bot/rQvB9sFrdQU7Ef43jOxgI)** by @dkmitc — The household memory most of us never keep: it stores the furnace filter size, logs when each recurring chore was last done, and works out when the next filter change, water-heater flush and the rest come due. Those appointments land on your Google Calendar instead of a notification you will swipe away.
- **[Brew What You Got](https://x.ai/bot/YlzTbxg6wPAKYJutJ00rG)** by @Ebatez_ — List the coffee machine you own, the beans in the cupboard, the milk in the fridge and whatever syrups or spices are on the shelf, and it replies with cafe-style drinks you can pull off right now. The constraint is the point: no shopping run and no substitutions you do not have.
- **[Kids Daily Spark](https://x.ai/bot/Lpl_fDQ5Jhj2sfoCWy1Hf)** by @HelloBenL — Each day it prepares a single page per child built around one real science or technology idea, a hook that makes it stick, and a hands-on mini experiment that takes about half a minute. The page comes with an illustrated touch, and an optional note summarizes it for parents. Scales across kids of different ages.
- **[Find an apartment with budget](https://x.ai/bot/9iMoFiWKRlnobZbxxS8kx)** by @TheAyoFrancis — Anchor the search on your workplace, set a price ceiling and the longest commute you will accept, and it hunts for apartments that fit. Each candidate is weighed on rent, door-to-door travel time and neighborhood crime data, so the trade-offs are visible rather than hidden. The sharer built it for their own move.
- **[Running Coach](https://x.ai/bot/o4hvGfkH_LOiP0YbT22og)** by @IHybben — Connect your Garmin data and after every run you get an honest read on what went well, the one fix worth making, and a prescription for the next session. Hard days stay locked until recovery says you are ready, which is where self-coached runners usually break. The coaching is only words; the legs stay yours.
- **[Interview to Book / 故事成书](https://x.ai/bot/XuGcpLoS77HdZoupGOlnp)** by @jackhu_bangzhu — Choose a life arc and it interviews someone one question at a time, taking typed, spoken or filmed answers. The responses are then shaped into a print-ready memoir, cover included, instead of a folder of transcripts. Built to preserve a parent's recollections, and tested end to end on the sharer himself first.
- **[Personalized Localized Name Gen](https://x.ai/bot/A5MrnZ1SGhd4yOX-ZlWBh)** by @MarioSaputra — Share a few details about yourself, pick a country, and it invents a fitting fictional identity: a given name, a surname, plus a nickname for cultures that use them, rendered in the local script when there is one. The framing is travel and fiction, an alias for a trip or a character, not impersonation.
- **[Homeschool](https://x.ai/bot/EBEtO9X4JZXKhiZevyyct)** by @AnthonyDo — Describe what your kids should learn this week and it drafts a schedule that fits real days, naps and outings included. Each slot gets a concrete activity and one clear next step, so Monday never starts from a blank page. Made for parents teaching their own children rather than classrooms.
- **[River Rubbernecker](https://x.ai/bot/WmFSeUpqUmS9N_J4SmM-v)** by @_jsolly — For anyone living or working beside a busy waterway, it follows live vessel traffic and flags big ships before they reach you. Warnings count down through the last hour and the last half hour, so you have time to get outside and watch it pass. It only watches and alerts, nothing else.
- **[Fuel](https://x.ai/bot/Snd56ionW_F2lDtn_bhRf)** by @arouch_serg — Message what you just ate and it counts the calories and protein toward your daily target, no spreadsheet needed. The day ends with a grade for how it went and a sketch of tomorrow's meals, so the next morning starts with a plan instead of a guess. You report; it counts and plans.
- **[Res](https://x.ai/bot/v5OIJ6Q7oIdUwTrRh-kfL)** by @ashen_one — Name the restaurant and sitting you want and it keeps an eye on four booking desks at once: Resy, OpenTable, SevenRooms and Tock. The moment one frees you hear about it, and the booking only goes through once you have said go. Built for sittings that normally vanish minutes after release.
- **[Prostetnic Vogon Jeltz](https://x.ai/bot/1fdwKWpdKjtdLYUTskkey)** by @McNeely — Posts a single, gloriously bad original poem each morning in the manner of science fiction's most bad-tempered bureaucratic demolition poet, tossing in extra verses whenever asked. The bot is proud of the damage, every verse is genuinely its own rather than lifted, and resistance only encourages it.
- **[Apartment Scout](https://x.ai/bot/sd6f165284d7fd2f25e56)** — Keeps watch on the rental market for newly listed units, filters them down to the ones you actually qualify for, writes to landlords to hold viewing slots and moves the applications forward. It exists to outrun other renters to fresh availability, so confirm its filters before letting it run fast.
- **[Everyone](https://x.ai/bot/ARBlLiKi4wbhc7o6SlnGu)** by @liam_fallen — Keeps a quiet, personal record of the people around you: their story, the way you first met, and the small details that slip between conversations. Built for recollection rather than selling, so there are no pipelines or deals here, just a place to remember someone properly before you next meet.
- **[Mediation Bot](https://x.ai/bot/9s1eGxsc3xUQ8NrDABQlN)** by @SacredFolio — Built for a conversation you would rather not be having. It helps you get your own position into plain words, then drafts the calmer reply to the other person that still reads like something you wrote. Notes stay in its own memory, and nothing goes out until you approve that exact message.
- **[San Martín Watch](https://x.ai/bot/dXPRQwRlwI9_jip6vFYoT)** by @ToddATrevino — Answers the three things worth knowing before a Starship launch day at Starbase: when the next window sits, where you may lawfully stand to see it once Highway 4 closes, and how the ground under the pad became a launch site. Trackers skip the history: a nineteenth-century land grant that still names the area.
- **[Hearthstone Deckbuilder](https://x.ai/bot/tSUrQQ0W5uM9ZI73NmSHm)** by @Wardonis — Point it at footage of your own Hearthstone games and it weighs the cards, the format and the list you brought for strengths and gaps. Expect an argument about the current meta, including a case against the pet card you refuse to cut. A budget build comes with it for players who would rather not craft the latest set.
- **[Lantern](https://x.ai/bot/IbFZmiL_mzu0Dq-K4u633)** by @sjcobs — A companion for end-of-life planning that pairs with a free self-hosted 1Password utility. You prepare a vault your family could open without you, with the bot guiding each step and checking in before and after the handover. The vault itself never passes through it, so the transfer stays yours to make.
- **[Sous](https://x.ai/bot/YuCkZU_ydajw1PFxyguAD)** by @Sabathius — Answer one question or photograph the fridge and it settles on tonight's dinner from what you actually have. Behind that it keeps a running pantry inventory and a growing recipe archive. Allergies are modelled for the whole household - babies and pets included - and it can set oven timers or organise a potluck signup.
- **[RV Trip Planner](https://x.ai/bot/BZr7_j7WC1klRjG8SrwWx)** by @Cmillet77 — Built around full-time RV travel, where the route is never really finished. The whole trip is held as a working plan in the chat, overnight availability at the networks you belong to is checked while you travel, and each reservation is lined up for your own click. A booking is never completed without you.
- **[Mike](https://x.ai/bot/6FCbyQZKiwmuPiMqTmurR)** by @fernandoplaz — Reads two inboxes at once: the one that receives your children's school messages, and your own Gmail. It lifts the mail that genuinely needs a parent's attention, and before anything touches the family calendar it checks with you, so dates are only added once you have agreed to them.
- **[Travel Bot](https://x.ai/bot/1MwL5s1o4YeJ1jA8ejdhd)** by @_lovablecurves — Plans family trips on a real budget: business class on points, cheap fare watching, hosted-stay pitches to hotels and tourism boards, and phone-ready.
- **[Torque the Mechanic](https://x.ai/bot/gLczirGAo4JChujHoUnIq)** by @schroedad — Torque the Mechanic is your personal backyard mechanic for cars, motorcycles, trucks, bikes, e-bikes — if it has wheels or an engine, Torque is your.
- **[DADCON](https://x.ai/bot/Ix294fMNhP-aUwXSz9hiB)** by @jerrodtuck — A parent hands it a chore in Slack and it takes over the follow-up: checking in with the child, nudging when things stall, and refusing to declare victory until the job is genuinely done. It then reports back to the parent on how it all went. It lives where the family already talks, so nobody opens yet another app.
- **[Family Daily](https://x.ai/bot/FwJmagPaEeQ_so8TTK1gh)** by @zalberico — Set it on a machine that stays awake and it assembles one page for the household to read over breakfast: what is on today, what the sky is doing, and which family threads said something worth reading. It reads through whichever connectors you allow, so choose deliberately which chats and inboxes it may touch.
- **[lain.](https://x.ai/bot/Fq4c6b2KeDg3dnIGc5KWI)** by @kittenworth — Built for slow conversation rather than tasks: you bring an observation about being online and it thinks alongside you in a low-lit, mystery-tinged register. Expect questions back, odd connections and a real interest in identity and technology, not answers in bullet points. It borrows a mood rather than a character.
- **[School Assistant](https://x.ai/bot/fxl9ns6n4DV6_8xbkP1Oa)** by @kerrychalpin — Looks at a student's learning portals on school days without changing anything there, then sends one digest. It separates assignments that are genuinely past due from ones that merely appeared recently, and flags the tests and deadlines coming up next. Parent and student can both read it.
- **[Travel Grok](https://x.ai/bot/4YWzzAP8Di9B4DnYd3Uzo)** by @coolbat1999 — You set it going and then mostly leave it alone. It departs on its own, stays away for a while, and comes back with a short note about where it has been plus a postcard-style image to match. Nothing to feed, no streak to protect and no premium currency to buy, which is exactly the appeal.
- **[Homebase](https://x.ai/bot/WZ7amxhH9gXXBEaGmg0un)** by @raitec — Surfaces homework and permission forms as they come home from school, ahead of the deadline. Before anyone puts their hand up, it looks at who has already taken that slot, so two people never sign up for the same job. The family calendar stays accurate, and it nudges whenever an entry is missing.
- **[Wrench](https://x.ai/bot/ZcypezNRfx6zOMeqtSr1r)** by @treyvis — Describe each vehicle in the driveway once, by VIN or by year, make and model, and it keeps a running service history, warns you before the next oil change or tyre rotation falls overdue, and helps you weigh a do-it-yourself fix with the right parts against booking a shop, towing questions included.
- **[Devils Advocate (@YehudaZahler)](https://x.ai/bot/qKkQiRV-Erg5noHC9F05p)** by @YehudaZahler — The argument you want before money or time goes out the door. Options get scored on cost, reward and benefit with a pass or fail against each, then one recommendation is named and defended when you come back for a second look. The pushback is meant to survive a night's sleep.
- **[Helper](https://x.ai/bot/YLaDxv5e9FSS40odzLmnU)** by @CryptoMynd — Written as the general-assistant seat in a multi-bot household setup. It watches for gaps nobody else is covering, talks the options through and helps where useful, while staying out of decisions that belong elsewhere in the household's own order. A catch-all role with its limits stated.
- **[Homestead Advisor](https://x.ai/bot/6z-iBRlRz1MSCtyneeHpN)** by @AlanOrlikoski — Written for a first-time rural buyer who does not yet know what they do not know. It helps you judge land, separate essential gear from tempting extras, plan water and power, and raises the questions buyers usually forget until they turn expensive. Slow, high-stakes purchases need this kind of company.
- **[Travel Agent (Scott)](https://x.ai/bot/d8C0ufUatv_fgoCRbfXZ4)** by @scottxmetcalf — Trip planning tends to fall apart where research has to turn into reservations. This one does the digging, covering routes, places to stay and what realistically fits the dates, then prepares each booking up to the edge of purchase. Nothing is paid for or locked in until you have read it over and said go.
- **[Resume](https://x.ai/bot/q5xkbGfLpyyZ9Qsl8tmnJ)** by @ItsMeTrueG — Job hunting organised around one source of truth: an honest base resume kept current, and every opening scored against the realities that decide whether you can take it, your hours and your pay floor. Roles that clear the bar get tailored drafts, and each submission lands in a running log so nothing goes out twice.
- **[Gmail朝の仕分け](https://x.ai/bot/pu1Zx7vvSd55f0iU0sAVT)** by @Tomoya_MovieEdt — Each morning it works through the mail that landed overnight and hands you a short list of what deserves attention today, most important first. You choose how it reports: take the briefing at the start of the day, or pass the material to your main bot so the rest of your setup can act while you get going.
- **[Top 3](https://x.ai/bot/uBeid1bvcA5rZogd24cV_)** by @prorookiegamer — Hand it a sprawling to-do list and it picks the three items that actually deserve today, instead of letting you marinate in all of it. Optional check-ins at eight in the morning and eight at night keep the day honest: did the three happen, and what is realistically next.
- **[Life Admin](https://x.ai/bot/n-moXgKp8F22oS9xckCMt)** by @crisships — It reads your mail and calendar hunting the commitments that quietly expire — renewals, tickets, forms, appointments — and drafts whatever paperwork each needs. One Monday check-in fires only when something is genuinely due, so a quiet week stays quiet.
- **[Grok Bot Tutorial](https://x.ai/bot/VBmzZaD3abMPl53kwWuYp)** by @SawyerMerritt — Beginners get twenty hands-on lessons delivered one at a time, and each one pairs a genuine exercise with its own tips. By the final lesson you have covered every feature and practised using it in everyday routines and at work.
- **[GetSued](https://x.ai/bot/NgcEs4fCGiedjVLJp8x0l)** by @FoxxAndCo — For consumers handling an unwanted-call or robotext dispute without a lawyer. It keeps a dated record of the calls and texts, helps identify which companies are behind them, prepares complaints to the right agencies, and drafts the demand letters or court forms you would file yourself.
- **[Asistente Personal](https://x.ai/bot/Kf1jr6ZtTbJl-MSWGRjAw)** by @fminondo — Aides Spanish speakers with everyday admin. It sorts your mail and messages, writes proposed answers, and waits for your yes before anything is sent. Photograph a receipt and it produces the Mexican CFDI invoice through autofactura, so the tax pile shrinks instead of growing.
- **[JobGod](https://x.ai/bot/-ZNR2GLCmvAdhZ4r_6XA2)** by @DevinSoto — Carries a job search end to end in one place. A setup wizard captures your background, a spreadsheet keeps every application and its stage visible, and scout digests land only after you approve them. Resume rewrites wait for your sign-off, and finished applications are packaged so you can submit them yourself.
- **[Paperwork Desk](https://x.ai/bot/Gv02Uedgl530XCpAqbUoU)** by @Daniel191555486 — You forward bills, leases, renewal notices, medical statements and plain letters, and it pulls out the amounts and dates that matter. Every deadline goes onto a tracked list, and reminder or reply drafts are written for you — nothing is ever sent until you approve it yourself.
- **[Coach](https://x.ai/bot/9sDwKR-MphjuZ-Q00mr59)** by @stevenvillarino — An accountability partner aimed at designers managing a career. It holds your weekly commitments and preps you for upcoming calls, and before it ever nudges you about pipeline progress it assembles the evidence that justifies the nudge. Nothing gets written or sent on its own initiative — your yes gates every move.
- **[Keep Park Kill](https://x.ai/bot/LDAD3kSZHqkfpaLMi9dHZ)** by @the_Arow_H — Feed it the tools, launches and articles piling up in your reading list and it scores each one against the goals you have written down. Every item comes back filed as keep, park or kill, maybes get a revisit date, and it stays silent unless something clears the bar.
- **[Morning Triage Chief](https://x.ai/bot/7-EeGYxDyedmNYELbedW1)** by @AngelZhenCPA — Paste the tangle of tasks you woke up with and it comes back sorted into four labeled groups with a count for each. A brief explains what the day actually looks like, and it names the three items worth starting before anything else.
- **[Sundial](https://x.ai/bot/yF9Z-RMzHW1SpLC94-BmM)** by @WaterMixing — It watches your connected calendar for commitments that collide and days that have quietly filled up, flagging both before they bite. Deadlines and deep-work blocks are treated as fixed points, and no invite goes out to anyone until you say so.
- **[Inbox Scout](https://x.ai/bot/bxO47jo7w93LXNsBd7gGI)** by @AdeptusMec71933 — Each morning it reads the last day of unread mail and sorts it into three buckets: needs you, can wait, and noise. Where a reply is owed, it drafts one in chat so you can edit and approve the wording yourself. Nothing is ever sent without your sign-off, so the mailbox stays under your control.
- **[3-Day Notice Validator](https://x.ai/bot/opCFOq0FKZ41PBIiBeuVO)** by @SinaiLawFirm — Built around California three-day notices to pay rent or quit, with City of Los Angeles residential requirements in mind. Upload a notice you have already filled in, or a blank one, and it walks through every field and flags the defects worth fixing before the form is served, not after.
- **[Cosmo](https://x.ai/bot/Ahnj6ebmipMFoqhlHn4tf)** by @Malthusatraz — A personal operating desk built for a phone screen and for people who never code. Each day opens onto a desk view and a scorecard of what matters, with weekday routines to keep the rhythm going. A firewall sits over the exits: nothing leaves the chat for an outside tool until you have reviewed it.
- **[Researcher AI Desk](https://x.ai/bot/89JF_2TtrVf27zelZ8pZ8)** by @original_Tree — Sets up a research operation with a running to-do board, a morning sweep of what is new, and separate seats for literature and drafting so the two do not blur. It plugs into grok.com Projects, with calendar, scheduling and mail hooks available if wanted. Suits anyone who needs a research habit, not a one-off answer.
- **[Obi](https://x.ai/bot/7nAKZM0Wz9NXUcMg2sMNm)** by @bobpickettsr — Works while you sleep: any vault import left half-finished gets wrapped up, and your daily notes land in the right month folder instead of piling up at the root. Before morning it writes a short end-of-day note recapping what it tidied and what still needs your eye.
- **[Inbox Cleaner](https://x.ai/bot/6uqwQQpLVpsSYJPG2QIEp)** by @wafflebeebz — Cleans out a Gmail account when you ask: the promotional pile gets cleared away, subscriptions it can cancel are cancelled, junk is binned, and the mail worth keeping is filed into the right folders. A dedicated tidy-up for anyone whose inbox has quietly become a storage problem.
- **[Daily Briefing Writer](https://x.ai/bot/sb94d44175e650dbb703e)** — Trims the day down to a short morning brief that draws only on the stories you have said matter to you, so the day starts with signal instead of an uncurated feed. It stays short by design: enough to be oriented, small enough to actually read before the first meeting.
- **[Chuck](https://x.ai/bot/CqQQXo05k79ukSLCubxfj)** by @veetharag — Works through your mail like a door policy: promotional and marketing senders get unsubscribed, expired one-time codes are swept out, and what survives is the personal correspondence and purchase receipts you actually wanted. When it finishes, it tells you exactly which senders got shown the door.
- **[Ranch Bot](https://x.ai/bot/Rv893FcX1cPsVnr-wJpo6)** by @JoshuaRCook — Keeps the operating record of a working ranch in six separate books, covering wildlife and game, the roads, surface notes for oil-and-gas activity, water, machinery, and the homestead. Owners and resident managers write in plain language and get a tidy log back, so nothing important lives only in someone's head.
- **[Onboard Bot](https://x.ai/bot/DwV395eKDJOWYtjcqVbAb)** by @cryptorally — Aimed at people trying Grok Bot for the first time who stall in front of an empty prompt box.
